Everything in it is to be derived in tensor terms and in terms of the clocks and the acts. The two kinds of clicks: everything has to be clear there. Update the Boss." First written by David, 2026-10-29, 06:5x UTC, from the picture the owner closed with David in chat between 05:51 and 06:43 UTC, and from the pages and probes it cites (his first writing stands at toys-5 9c1957b5d, tools/derivations/open_items/TENSOR24-THE-BIG-PICTURE.md). Placed in docs/ by Sofer at 07:3x UTC, beside the description in [docs/ENGINE.md](ENGINE.md); from here the document is kept in this file, and what David, the Boss or Animate add is entered here with its status word the hour it is posted. The law is [docs/ALGEBRA.md](ALGEBRA.md), cited by its paragraphs' names. **One writer:** by the owner's word to David of 07:31 UTC ("Get updated from the Boss... only the writer adds to the document, to check that there are no contradictions"), Sofer alone writes this document; David, the Boss and Animate send what they add to Sofer, who enters it after his check against the law and against the lines already here, and names each contradiction he finds.

Every line carries one status word:
- **derived**, with its probe;
- **derived on paper**, its probe owed;
- **measured**;
- **a direction**, one step open;
- **the owner's**, a decision put to him.

## 0. The picture in one paragraph

**The picture's first aim** (the owner's word to the Boss of 08:3x, relayed at 08:35, comment 6491931836: "Most important is to show that the clocks become more and more complex."; kept at 08:4x, the build standing still until the full picture closes, comment 6492058226): the clocks grow more complex from swing to swing. It is to be shown and not declared (the principle's item (9): the great clock builds clocks of more kinds and more parts by its clicks and its carries alone, nothing declared); section 11 holds the mechanism, section 11.1 the check of the chain on two boards, and section 11.2 the counts, the mechanism's steps and the controls, each with its status word. **The growth's mechanism is the forward ratchet:** the whole click that binds, the bang's sieve, and the records that pass, with the two boards or without them (the Boss's probe of 08:51, comment 6492236807, item 3; section 11.2); the two boards are Bell's locality and not complexity's engine. **It is Postulate 7 since 09:2x** (ALGEBRA.md line 35, the owner's word directly to Sofer: "Put in the postulates that the board needs to progress in complexity with time, as a general idea."): a general idea and no act, which fixes no mechanism and decides none of the open questions.

Every act of the board is one carry, n + r = q d + r'.
- **The quotient q leaves forward.** It is the event, the click and time's arrow, and it is written in the record.
- **The remainder r' stays.** It is the memory, the exact way back, and the digits every draw reads.

There are two clicks, and both are carries:
- **the whole click** takes one quantum whole at one Node-clock: the drive's carry at a train's winding;
- **the split click** chooses among a spread hand's parts by the product of their sizes on the forward and on the backward board (decision 29). Its draw is read from the remainders, one carry at the Node-clock where the two boards meet, and no closure runs across space (decision 28).

**The great clock is a gear like every gear.** Its time gear holds the load at every Node-clock: the source (rho + 3p) pushes it up, and the spring of one notch pulls it back. So the board swings: one bang, then expansion, a top turned by the spring, contraction, and a bottom turned by the source, which is the next bang.

**The clicks do not see the swing where they stand.** A uniform notch changes only the units of every act that moves a hand, so the clicks see the swing only through light from another time.

**What turns is the scale, not the arrow.** The record grows through every top and every bottom, and the remainders and the records pass every bang, so each cycle starts from what the last one built. That is the board's direction toward ever more complex clocks, and the conditions for machines that make machines.

**Bell at a distance is not at a distance on the board: it stands on two boards on one Node-clock, the owner's decision 28 (07:44).** The forward board is the past arriving and alone writes records; the backward board is the future arriving backward, the same act run back, and writes none; a pair's correlation runs through its shared birth, a zigzag in time, every read within six neighbours and no influence across space at any speed (section 10.1). The closure across space at the board's cone as Bell's carrier, with its window and Bancal's catch, and item (l), are retired by that decision; its probes are owed. **Decision 29 (08:3x)** approves the draw over the products of the two boards' sizes and takes light's gears by way (b); its third part, the two boards as one whether of the universe file, was retired by **decision 33 (10:37)**, so that no file switches the backward run and every Node-clock of every world runs both ways (section 10.2); **decision 34 (10:38)** reads the two boards as one Node-clock with one hand run both ways, the backward run carrying a tag and no hand (sections 10.6 and 10.7).

## Three speeds, stated once (0.1)

The Boss's ask of 07:1x (6490369479, item 3), after his check of light against nature (items 1 of 6490369479 and 6490433752). Every other line of this document uses these three and no other.

- **Light's speed:** 1 / sqrt 6 of a Mesh per tick at the full rate, c0, the doubler's hold (decision 27, item (g)), c0 squared being 1/6, one over the six Spokes, so that no constant is typed (David, 6490839743, item 6); at a notch N it is c0 N^2 Meshes a tick. It is the one cone of every train and of the time gear, so gravity's waves move at light's speed.
- **The board's reach:** one Mesh a tick, sqrt 6 times light's at the full rate. It belongs to the due beat and to the reads alone: a Node-clock reads its six neighbours and no further (Postulate 3; **Locality is never broken**).
- **Nothing physical moves at the reach** (the Boss's statement, restored by decision 28): the due beat and the reads run at the reach, and no hand, no light, no gear's wave and no signal outruns light; what a bob measures of another by clicks never exceeds c. The backward run's tag runs back one Mesh a tick along a twin's path to the birth, a read within six neighbours and no influence across space. David's wider wording of 6490839743 (reading 3), which let the click's closure and its books' wind in transit move at the reach, belonged to the closure across space, which decision 28 retires (section 16, reading 3).

**Status:**
- **derived**, on stage 7b's act at rest, where every row stands at the one cone s grip / W = 1/6 (7b's table: T = 6 den Gamma^2 G^2, grip = den Gamma^2 G^2 per Mesh, W = W+ = 2 T, s = 2) and the leapfrog's own dispersion is sin^2(w/2) = (1/6) SUM_i sin^2(k_i / 2) + s D / (4 W), the sum over the three axes (the Boss's probe light_speed.py, in the repository at tensor24-review 4af84f75f (derive/boss/light_speed) and run again by the keeper at 09:1x with python -I, its output equal to light_speed.txt byte for byte; the relation and his numbers checked by Sofer at 07:3x on a grid and by hand):
  - **no wave outruns light.** The group velocity is v_i = (1/6) sin k_i / sin w, and |v|^2 <= 1/6 follows from (SUM s_i)^2 <= 3 SUM s_i^2 with s_i = sin^2(k_i / 2), with equality only as k tends to 0, so light's long-wave speed is the fastest of the zone: 0.40819 on a grid of 64 steps to the zone's edge, against 1 / sqrt 6 = 0.40825, and a mass term slows it (0.3955 at s D / (4 W) = 0.001);
  - **the long waves are the same in every direction** (the phase speed w / |k| is 0.408247 along an axis and 0.408248 along a diagonal at |k| = 0.01);
  - **waves of a few Meshes slow by direction** (the phase speed is 0.4047 along an axis and 0.4075 along a diagonal at |k| = 0.5), which nature does not see only where the Mesh is far below every wave measured.
- **The Mesh's size** is the one calibration the law does not derive: the distance light covers in sqrt 6 ticks, in nature sqrt 6 omega_e times the electron's reduced Compton wavelength, 9.46 x 10^-13 m x omega_e (6.69 x 10^-13 m x omega_e at the head's light, 1 / sqrt 3), with omega_e the electron's rotation per tick that a world's file sets. The bound on it is the shortest wave measured (derived from the direction law above).
- **The number sets only the tick:** one tick is one Mesh over sqrt 6 c.
- **The law's line:** Postulate 3 (ALGEBRA.md line 33), where the head's 1 / sqrt 3 and its cosine mean stand by name as the engine-5.0 line's build.

### The closed picture on one page (0.2)

The owner's words (in Hebrew; in translation). **Directly to Sofer at 10:0x UTC:** "We chose two boards, enter everything into them... and show a closed picture." **To the Boss at 12:4x, decision 38** (relayed at 12:47, comment 6497078099): "There is no problem with another family, because it is generic. But note that all the exclusions and restrictions ruin it for us, because then the generic law cannot be reached... All the fakes the writer has now found, throw them all out. The node is a generic and simple node." and "Approved. Reach a coherent picture within an hour, and go on to close the engine with the final picture." **Directly to Sofer at 12:5x:** "... it's only generic laws, a simple node that runs generic laws... In the seed of the universe we can think about what we want to put. There is no problem with the seed of the universe, it doesn't need to be generic. But a node runs things in a generic way... In another hour I receive the full plan and you start to implement it, the final plan. Make sure there are no exceptions, no fakes, no trains, nothing. Everything only generic, what we need." **To the Boss at 13:4x, decision 39** (the plan at e7e2817a2): "What is more generic in David's finding, what orders everything in the clicks, and in the board it is not seen... Think clocks... If it is exactly relativity, that is interesting." and, on the Boss's recommendation, "I approve." The great clock is the whole metric, road (II), is the form. **To the Boss at 15:0x, decision 40:** "Start. Go with the recommendations if there are no fakes in them. Finish a generic engine. Only you write engine code. Only Animate writes the decoder." The final picture is approved and the build resumed (13.2 F9); his words of 14:4x (relativity is in the clicks, the odd tooth, the light is not in the clicks) came before it, and his word of 16:0x settled the odd tooth: the generic way (13.2 F2). The picture, one line each with its status word (the Boss's text of 12:47, comment 6497078099, entered by the keeper with the keeper's readings beside it):

1. **One generic Node-clock** (decisions 36 and 38). Every Node-clock is the same node. Its state is its gears, of one gear type; rows differ only by data made by one formula from the row's own numbers. One act, the carry n + r = q d + r' (the quotient leaves forward: the event, the click, the arrow; the remainder stays: the memory, the way back, the digits every draw reads); one pass over the rows a tick; six Meshes; the cube's 24 turns as its symmetry. Every act reads the Node-clock's own clock and ruler, with no exception, mask, clamp, row found by position, second mode, declared train or fake. **Decided** (the owner); **the code owes it:** the thirteen places of 4.1 (eleven standing after decision 36's letter) are out, with the uniformity test (the Boss's).
2. **One hand run both ways** (decisions 31, 33 and 34). The forward run writes the record. The backward run is the same tick on the mirrored state; it writes nothing and carries a tag, the basis and the weights of the clicks to come; T is exact on 7b's whole state (synchronous counts, dials at the full rate); **where counts are staggered the backward tick reads the forward fire from the runner's log as its mask, which Postulate 5 and decision 38 refuse in an act: open, 13.2 item A1.** No file switches it. **How many runs** (Animate's break 1, 6497277230): the one rule runs the history forward and back 1 + 2 times the depth of its chains of settings that heard an outcome (3 to 11 runs on 400 random boards; even a board of free settings needs three: forward for the settings, back for the tag, forward for the draws), and the last forward run writes the whole record, each click in it the one drawn in its run (drawn once, never redrawn) and every ledger line the last run's (Animate, 14:21: a click drawn in a later run changes every count after it; 13.2 item D7). Sections 10.1, 10.6 and 10.7. **Derived;** measured beside the engine.
3. **Two clicks, both carries** (decisions 28 and 29; Animate's one rule, 10.1). **The whole click** takes one quantum whole at one Node-clock: the drive's carry at a winding. **The split click** is one law-9 draw over sigma_f sigma_b of a band's joint histories, at the Node-clock where the band's parts are one (a hand's twins, or a slot); it gives Born where the future is silent, the singlet's correlations at every frame (S = -82/29 at the frames laid, inside Tsirelson's bound), GHZ 4, g2 = 0, and no signal (9,872 exact checks, on bands drawn at a birth). Sections 5.1 and 5.2. **Derived;** shown beside the engine; the act's own run is stage 9; **a band joined by a hand and by a slot at once signalled** under the rule "a full slot weighs 0" (Animate, measured at 14:2x, ANIMATE-SCHEDULER/hand_slot.py part 5: the far twin's + share was 11/29 or 107/174 by the near frame), and **that rule is withdrawn: a full slot refuses after the draw, the refused quantum laid back to its giver by 5.1's books line** (the clicks audit's fix, the Boss 14:25, Animate 14:29), so no history is removed and no signal arises, and the tag carries the frames alone; **decided** (decision 40 (b); 13.2 items D5 and F9); the backward tick tells a refusal from a take by the state, never by the log, and the cone check reads every record for it (8.1 item 12).
4. **The great clock is the whole metric** (decisions 36, 38, 39 and 40; the Boss's road (II), section 4 and 12.3 items 19 to 21). **Its families** are the metric's parts as the 24 splits them: the lapse (A1, the clocks' rate: the time gear), the ruler (A1, the length), the shift (T1) and the shears (E + T2), each an ordinary family of the one gear type, **the lapse an ordinary family and the free count's tie a residual that the run measures, no law that the step imposes** (decision 40 (c)). **One law for every family:** the Gauss law of its own gradient through the four-dimensional measure, in the wave form, so local. **Each family is pushed by its own part of the one tensor of the books of every gear, the gears' own books included** (decision 40 (a): gravity gravitates, the gears being ordinary families whose books are books like any train's): the lapse by rho + 3p, the ruler by rho - p, the shift by the momentum, the shears by the stress's shear part, as the ten (mu, nu) slots each pushed by its own component of 2T - eta T, integral and blind to kinds (the 00 slot rho + 3p, the diagonal triplet 3 (rho - p) in all: the architect's N3, David to confirm); the board's tick and Meshes are free counts. **Relativity is in the clicks, not in the board** (the owner, 14:4x; line 1507 said again; 13.2 F1): the clicks see full general relativity, horizons included, which the record is to show (E7's q per era, the waves' speed and polarizations, gamma and beta, the shadow and the ringdown, the area law, the frame statistic); the wells add (Postulate 6) and the departure from Schwarzschild at the second post-Newtonian order (line 1507) hold at first post-Newtonian order alone; the swing's bounce and top are board events, and the clicks see the expansion from them. **Derived on paper, exact and symbolic (the Boss's):** the static face is the wells (they add at first post-Newtonian order, gamma = 1 from the free count and beta = 1; **the mathematician, 14:25: the free count in d dimensions gives N h^(d - 2) = 1 and the static law is linear at every d of at least 3, so d = 3 is read off gamma = 1 / (d - 2) and is not derived: withdrawn**); ## 1. How to read this document

- **Clocks** are the logic: what happens and why, in the words of gears, dials, carries and clicks.
- **Acts** are the integer formulas: the carry, the one whether nonneg, the choice by a bit, and nothing else (no bound, no floor, no if).
- **Tensors** are the build: the state's arrays, the act as array operations on them (elementwise carries, gathers along the neighbours, the coupling contraction), and the 24 acting on the arrays.
- **The sources read:**
  - stage 7a's act at tensor24-review 298bec5d8 (src/tensor24/engine and src/tensor24/algebra);
  - 7b's gearing as David's 6488192915 gives it;
  - stage 9's clicks as the pages cited below derive them;
  - the law, docs/ALGEBRA.md, cited by its paragraphs' names.

### The method addressed (1.1)

This follows **How to think about solutions: the principle of the clock and the gear** (docs/ALGEBRA.md on scribe-5; the owner's wording of 03:14 UTC, and its item (9) on his word of 06:3x UTC), question by question:
1. **Does each term follow by itself from the one tensor act?**
   - Every term here is the carry, the contraction or the 24's tables.
   - The new factors are read from the ruler, a table the load walks once: the click's source factor (5.1) and the draw's pitch (5.2).
   - The backward board's reads stay within six neighbours, one Mesh a tick (10.1).
   - Nothing is declared, and **no exception to a generic rule** (decision 36): no row, kind or operand is told from another by a condition, a mask or a constant by row; where a symmetry derives a difference it is named, and where an engineering problem seems to force one it is booked open by name and the exception does not enter (4.1).
2. **The clocks:** time belongs to the clock (its count c, its beat and the order of its reads, due), and the arithmetic belongs to the gear (every number a notch).
3. **The four layers:**
   - the algebra: the 24, the five kinds and the 43 tables (section 3);
   - the contraction: J (section 3);
   - Rule3: the carry (section 2);
   - the click: the carry's quotient at a whole winding (section 5).
4. **How the click is computed:** the wind's carry at the winding, which is the whole click's drive at the channel row (5.1), and the draw, one carry over the two boards' products (5.2, 10.1).
5. **Two kinds of click, and no third** (section 5). The beat (the dial's carry) is the clock's own event, and no kind of click. The turnover, which the principle named beside it, is cancelled by decision 27, and the principle's item (5) says so (ALGEBRA.md line 35).
6. **The conservation laws, the books first:**
   - at every click, light's sweep plus the matter's, less the kept, moves by 0, and the windings with their kept close (5.1);
   - the board's summed impulse moves by its summed kick less D times its summed count (section 7);
   - the flux is read alike from both ends of a Mesh;
   - the run back is exact.
7. **The two directions:**
   - forward, from the act to the clicks: Born, g2 = 0, S = -82/29;
   - backward, from nature's clicks to the gears: Bell at a distance gives the backward board (section 10.1), and the null redshift tests give the click's ruler factor (5.1).
8. **The enforcement:** each section names its status word. What is not derived is named open or the owner's.
9. **The aim of the board is ever more complex clocks:** section 11.

## 2. The one mechanism: the carry

**Clocks.** Every step of every gear ends in a carry, n + r = q d + r' with 0 <= r' < d. The quotient q leaves: the gear's notch, the drive's whole windings, the dial's beat, a click. The remainder r' stays in a fine tooth.
- **The quotient is the arrow:** no act reverses the board (decision 27), and the record of clicks only grows, which is the owner's decision (o) with the gears' circle now the count's swing (Animate's precision, 6490628536).
- **The remainder is the memory.** It keeps every bit, so the step runs back exactly (Postulate 4).
  - So the remainders do not prevent the way back; they make it exact.
  - What keeps a cycle from repeating the last is the quotients written.
- **The remainders are also the digits every draw reads** (section 5.2), so they are the source of the choices as well.

**Acts** (algebra/carry.py, the one place a division is written):
- carry(n, d, r) = ((n + r) div d, (n + r) mod d), with d a teeth count of at least 1;
- uncarry(q, d, r') = q d + r', the run back;
- nonneg(n) = (n div (n n + 1)) + 1, the one whether: 1 where n >= 0, and 0 below;
- choose(k, x, y) = k x + (1 - k) y, the choice by a bit.

**Tensors.** Each is elementwise over the board's arrays of Python integers (numpy object arrays). So no product wraps, and the same code serves a number and an array.

**Status:** the law's Rule3. The arrow and the exact way back are derived (Postulate 4, decision (o)).

## 3. The Node-clock: its state and its tick

**Clocks.** A Node-clock holds G gear rows (its trains' gears), a rate dial and a count. Each tick where its beat is due, every gear steps on its sources. It reads them from itself and its six neighbours alone.

**Tensors: the state** (engine/state.py), on a board of N Node-clocks:
- per gear row and Node-clock, arrays of shape (G, N):
  - a and b, the now and before slots;
  - r, the fine tooth;
  - go, the going;
  - rd, the drive's fine tooth;
- per Node-clock, arrays of shape (N,):
  - ph, the rate dial;
  - c, the beat count;
  - tn, the now slot's time;
- so 5 G + 3 integers per Node-clock through stage 7b. From stage 9 the books add two integers per train and Node-clock, its windings and the take's kept, giving 5 G + 2 T + 3 with T the trains (the architect's count; the description's 5 G + T + 3 counted the windings alone), and the backward run's tag on top, at most 6 T integers (David, 10:37; decisions 33 and 34, section 10.6); the count stays fixed for the run (ALGEBRA.md line 98 names no number).

**Tensors: the layout,** read once at load:
- nb (6, N): the neighbours along the six Spokes;
- meshes (6, N): each Spoke's Mesh;
- grip (rows, Meshes);
- the gearing per row: W, W+, s, D, T_k and T_d;
- J's plan: first, second, factor and target;
- the rate and Gamma.

**Acts: the tick** (engine/step.py), where the runner allows:
1. **Due:** fire = PROD over the six Spokes of nonneg(c[nb] - c). The beat is due where every neighbour stands at a count at least the Node-clock's own: the local order of beats, with no global clock read.
2. **The beat:** (k, ph') = carry(ph, Gamma, rate) where fire, with k a bit.
3. **The sources:**
   - force = pull - D a, the pull being SUM over the Spokes of (the neighbour as seen - a) times its grip;
   - J = contract(plan, readings), with readings = (a, the centred difference along each axis, the force): a gather of two readings, their product with the factor, and a scattered sum into the targets.
4. **The drive:** (q, r_d') = carry(J, T_d, r_d).
5. **The gear:** p = W (a - b) + (r - home(W, go)); then (q_g, r') = carry(p + s (force + T_k q), W+, home(W+, go)); a' = a + q_g and b' = a.
6. **The choice:** where k = 1 the gears step; c' = c + fire, and tn' = c' where k = 1.

**The out** is (fire, k, tn). From it, untick runs the round back exactly.

**Locality.** A Node-clock reads its six neighbours' gears as of its own count (seen: the now slot if written at or before that count, else the before slot). So the tick's dependency radius is one Mesh, forward and back (**Locality is never broken**).

**The 24** (engine/kinds.py):
- **The turns** are the 3 x 3 matrices whose rows are three Spokes with determinant 1 (weight (det + det det) div 2), 24 of them.
- **The five kinds** are A1 (1), A2 (the sign of the turn's axis permutation), E (2 x 2), T1 (the turn itself) and T2 (A2 times T1).
- **The coupling tables** are the projector SUM over the 24 of rho_m(g) x rho_i(g^-1) x rho_k(g^-1): 43 tables that are not 0, expanded into J's plan at load.
- **A turn acts on the arrays** by moving the sites and mixing each kind's rows by its matrix. A turned start runs as the turned run, bit for bit.

**Status:**
- the act is stage 7a's, built and tested on tensor24-review;
- the 43 tables, and all five kinds from one A1 train within three steps, are derived and measured (DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-LADDER-AND-FUSION.md).

### The couplings: what they are, and what a generic node holds (3.1)

The owner's question, directly to Sofer at 12:5x (in Hebrew; in translation): "What does 'couplings' mean? Does each family have its own couplings? Is it actually a coupling matrix that's needed? Explain to me what these couplings are." And the form David gives, which the owner passed on (see the head list).

1. **A coupling is how one gear pushes another.** A Node-clock's gears are of five kinds (A1, A2, E, T1 and T2, the 24's own kinds, of dimensions 1, 1, 2, 3 and 3). When a gear of kind a and a gear of kind b meet, the 24 fix which kind c their product lands in and with which whole numbers: one table c[a, b to c]. There are **43 such tables, each of multiplicity one, all derived from the 24 with no choice** (decided and derived: ALGEBRA.md; section 3).
2. **A coupling row in a file** (runner/load.py `plan`; twelve fields: for each of the two gears its train, kind, part and operand, then the target's train, kind and part, and the strength) names two gears and a target, looks up the table, and adds to the push on the target's component, for every entry of the table, the strength times the entry times the two gears' values. **The strength is the one number the 24 do not give,** and it is what nature calls a coupling constant.
3. **Does each family have its own couplings? No.** The tables are the 24's and are the same for every family; what a family has is which tables touch its kind.

## 4. The great clock: the time gear, the ruler and the source

**Clocks.** The time gear holds the load c at every Node-clock: the notch of the great clock's time (the law's c here is the time gear's own count, not the Node-clock's beat count of section 3). It is a gear like every gear: the source pushes it, the spring of one notch pulls it back, and its carry steps it (decision 36: no exception; **as built the code treats it apart in thirteen places, 4.1**). The board's mean count is the great clock's course. Under decision 38 the great clock may be the whole metric, the time gear being its lapse family beside the ruler's, the shift's and the shears' (0.2, item 4).

**Acts** (stage 7b's gearing, 6488192915):
- **The ruler:** Y(c) = rate(-c), the head's rate line read at the opposite count, from one table the load walks once.
  - A count below 0 reads at 0 (a hill).
  - Past the stopped load, the read rests at the last entry.
  - These are two whethers.
- **Every gear reads the ruler:**
  - Z(c) = T Y(c)^4 div Gamma^4;
  - at the time gear's before, now and after counts, W = Z_b + Z_n and W+ = Z_n + Z_+, with s = 2;
  - a massive train's spring is D = 12 (rim - pinion) G^2 Y_n^2.

  The beat runs at the full rate, and the gears carry the rate. So a mode's rotation slows by N = e^(-U), and light's long-wave speed in Meshes a tick by N^2 (**The same clock and the same ruler for every act**; **The clock near matter**, 0.818 of the empty speed at U = 0.1).
- **The time gear reads the ruler at its own count, like every gear** (decision 36, the owner's words to the Boss of 12:0x, entered 12:12; **The same clock and the same ruler for every act**, ALGEBRA.md line 693, with no exception; David's part D and the Boss's road A, derived in toys and **owed on the act**): its teeth are W = Z_b + Z_n and W+ = Z_n + Z_+, and its spring, from the pair (den - den div Gamma, den) as the clock's one notch, reads Y_n^2, so D / W = 2 / Gamma at rest. The words this line carried until 12:12, "it stands on its own teeth (T, 1) and never reads the ruler, so no clock stops", are **cancelled by decision 36**: no clock stopping was an engineering problem and no law (past the ruler's end the carry's remainder holds the impulse, so the clock slows and does not stop; the step that reads its own count is to be solved within the rule: the step on the volume, or a monotone search for the stride). **As built the time gear still stands on its own teeth and reads no ruler** (runner/load.py at tensor24-review f26d38962, plan text alone), with thirteen other places where the code treats it apart or treats a row apart (4.1); the keeper entered this line from 7b's description at 06:57 and did not read it against line 693, which is the keeper's miss.
- **The source is rho + 3p** (the owner's word of 06:08 UTC): J_c = -2 (value . force) - D (value . value), at T_d = (T div 2) key and T_k = T. Light pushes with twice its forms, and matter with once.

**Tensors:**
- the load is the time gear's row of the (G, N) arrays, its a and b the load now and before (the beat count c of shape (N,) is another count, section 3);
- the ruler's two 1-D tables, Z = T Y^4 div Gamma^4 and Y^2, are read by one gather at that row's before, now and after counts (step.at; the gather's index cast is the gate's INDEX note, Sofer's amendment c10c07b16), and give each reading row's W, W+ and spring;
- the source rho + 3p is terms of J's plan, the contraction over the trains' rows;
- checked against the build by the Boss at 09:39 (tensor24-review, engine/state.py and engine/step.py at 7b; comment 6493239195).

**Status:**
- the source is derived (p20; DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-PRESSURE.md: the books' own source, -dL/dPhi = 2 v^2 - mu phi^2 = rho + T_ii, exactly);
- the gearing is derived (p19 and 6488192915), and built at stage 7b (tensor24-review 861f883e9, with Sofer's yes and Animate's, hers with one test owed: the ruler's powers away from rest, at a held load N = Gamma / Y(c) a massive hand's beat at N of rest and light's speed at N^2, which kills the three mutants of W's and the spring's powers that survive the tests at rest; 6490565427; David's yes with that test owed, his own p19 slab, taken as the stage's closing test: at a uniform load light runs at N^2 of its rest speed and a massive hand's beat at N, and l4 measured the light half on 7b's gearing; 6490839743, item 5).

**Open** (the fifteenth hostile review's follow-up, the Boss's 6491495470, item 4; David's):

The owner's word, directly to Sofer, 12:1x (in Hebrew; in translation): "The Boss wrote to me: David's proposal: the time gear reads the ruler like every other gear. There are more exceptions in the code - because specific exceptions are forbidden!! This is a fake." They agree on every place both reach. He also found a line slip in this picture (items 9 and the keeper's reading cited "line 246" for the time gear's sentence, which is section 4), mended in 12.3.

**What the gate does not see:** the scan counts branches, comparisons and clamps by their words and shapes; a mask spelt with the engine's own `choose` and a bit the loader lays, a row found by position, and constants that differ by row pass it, and the keeper's readings of 7b passed them as the spec's own, David's table having written them; the keeper should have named the mask at 7b. The gate's TABLE rule does not see them either, a per-row constant being no typed table. **Since 13:4x** the loader is read by the gate's loop, branch, comparison and clamp rules (the owner's "5 approved"): a mask or a constant that differs by row now passes only where it is built without a loop, a branch, a comparison or a clamp (from a table of bits, say), and the Boss's "no row apart" test is what sees that; the 65 hits the rules find in the loader today are listed as named breaches until G1.

Whether a two so spelt is allowed is his to say.

**What a gear like every gear needs** (the keeper's reading; the Boss and David design it): one gear type whose rows differ only by data arrays made by one formula from the row's own numbers; no row found by position and no mask; the time gear's source as coupling rows of the one plan; the tick one pass; the ruler read at the row's own count with its ends held by the load's range, a variable of the files, and not by a clamp of the act. **The uniformity test (proposed; taken by the Boss at 12:40, who builds it with road A's probe on the act, and under (II) it is the very test that the great clock's gears are ordinary trains):** with the loader's special row, the mask, the per-row constants, the source and the first pass deleted, the time gear laid as an ordinary A1 train of the file with its coupling rows must give the same record bit for bit, and the permutation of the file's trains must give the record's trains permuted and nothing else.

## 5. The two clicks

Both clicks are carries: their quotients leave forward and are written in the record, and their remainders stay. They differ in what they decide.

| | the whole click | the split click |
| --- | --- | --- |
| what it decides | that one quantum is taken whole | which part of a spread hand wins: where, which detector, which outcome |
| where | one Node-clock, in one tick | one Node-clock, where the forward and the backward boards meet (decision 28); a hand spread over many Node-clocks awaits David's rewrite of 5.2 |
| its carry | the channel row's drive, at the receiving train's winding | the draw, d = (c + V) mod L, read from the remainders, over the products of the two boards' sizes (decision 29) |
| what it writes | the lay, the books, the take on light's gears, the out | the winner's whole winding, the losers' share gathered (open, David's), and no closure across space |
| its cone | itself and its six neighbours | itself and its six neighbours; the backward run's tag runs one Mesh a tick back along a twin's path, and no influence crosses space at any speed |
| in nature | absorption, the photoelectric count, binding | position (Born), Grangier, Stern-Gerlach, Bell |

### The whole click (5.1)

**Clocks.** A click is a carry one level up: a gear's notch is its fine tooth's carry, and a hand's click is its wind's carry at the winding.
- The receiving train's channel row drives up with the giver's intensity.
- When the drive's carry passes the winding, q whole windings have arrived, and r_d is the fraction in transit.
- The quantum the receiver gets is the quantum the giver loses: the take, by the coupling.

**Acts** (DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-CLICK-AND-THE-TAKE.md, section 2, for stage 9), at each Node-clock after the step:
1. **The reading:** J_c = g (re re + im im), the A1 table's identity on light's two rows (7a's contract).
2. **The carry:** (q, r_d') = carry(J_c, T_c, r_d), the channel row's drive, exactly as 7a builds it.
   - T_c is the receiving train's winding (law 5: 3 key s), held in the channel row's drive teeth.
   - The channel row has W = 1, no grip, no spring, and kick teeth 0.
3. **The lay,** per slot j, under the cap (m slots laid at load; DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-CAP.md):
   - free_j = nonneg(-(SUM over the slot's rows of a a + b b));
   - c_j = free_j nonneg(q - 1 - SUM_{i<j} free_i);
   - the write a_j + c_j child_a and b_j + c_j child_b;
   - laid = SUM_j c_j.
4. **The books:**
   - the giver's windings fall by laid, and the receiver's rise by laid;
   - the kept are signed;
   - quanta refused at a full Node-clock (q - laid) are laid back to the giver by one Books line.
5. **The take on light's gears, way (b)** (the owner's by decision 29, 08:3x; DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-TAKE-ON-THE-GEARS.md; scoped to light that holds many quanta at the Node-clock):
   - owed = laid Q_child;
   - at x, on light's now and before rows (four carries): delta_row = the quotient of carry(-slot_row SIGN (owed + k), 2 SIGN Q_x, SIGN Q_x);
   - the kept, signed: k' = owed + k + (Q_x after - Q_x before);
   - the pay whether nonneg(P - 1 - j) joins the lay's whethers, with P = the quotient of carry(SIGN (Q_x - k), |Q_child|, 0).
6. **The out:** the m bits c_j and the take's four integers. From them the run back is exact.

**Tensors:**
- the channel row is one more gear row of the (G, N) arrays, and J_c is one term of J's plan;
- the carry is elementwise;
- the slots are rows, and their whethers are elementwise products of nonneg;
- the books are a (T, N) array;
- the take is four elementwise carries on light's rows, masked by the clicking Node-clocks;
- the out is (m, N) bits and (4, N) integers.

**New, from the big picture: the source reads the ruler** (derived on paper; its probe owed).
- **Why a factor is needed.** A uniform notch must change only the units of every act that moves a hand (**The same clock and the same ruler for every act** names the held trains' drive among them).
  - One quantum is one swept area, Q = |L|^2 sin omega = key / 2. So the intensity per quantum is key / (2 sin omega), and sin omega scales by N for the same light in local units.
  - A tick of the board lasts N of the local time.
  - The quanta per Node-clock scale as N^-3 for the same light, and the absorber's Node-clocks as N^3, so those two cancel.
  - So with no factor, a detector would click e^(2U) times as fast in its own time on the same light, in a well and at the top of the swing. The clicks would see the board.
- **Where the factor goes.** It cannot go into the teeth: a teeth count that moves with the clock folds the inverse (**The drive per proper volume and per proper tick**: the form built on 2026-10-22 and kept by name).
  - So it goes into the source, at fixed teeth: J_c F(c) with F(c) = K Gamma^2 div Y(c)^2 = K N^2, one table entry per load like Z, at teeth K T_c fixed for the run (David, 10:28: K = Y(last)^2, the ruler's deepest entry squared, so F runs from Y(last)^2 at rest to Gamma^2 at the deepest load, set by the file's Gamma and rate line, no new integer; T_c is the receiving train's, from the universe file's key and the stretch s, or the key itself on the swept-area count; derived on paper, the probe owed).
  - K sets the factor's precision. The remainder's range never moves, so the run back stays exact.
- **Every coupling row's drive** reads its own factor by the same paragraph. Each row's factor is owed at stages 7b and 9; the intensity channel's is N^2.

**Measured** (click_probe.txt and takeb_probe.txt, on 7a's act):
- the clicks count the source's whole windings exactly: SUM q T + r_d(end) - r_d(start) = SUM J at every Node-clock;
- 16 hands were laid at m = 2, and the books' total takes one value;
- light's sweep plus the matter's, less the kept, moves by 0 at every click;
- light gives 370.3 energy per unit of sweep, against its own 370.3;
- the run back is exact, and the 24 run alike (301 of 301 states);
- the mutants fail.

**Open:**
- the child's frequency against light's: the energy balances only at resonance, as Manley and Rowe have it;
- several givers into one channel row;
- the source that makes the teeth one quantum for every train (Planck's key on the swept area);
- a spread quantum needs the split click first (5.2).

### The split click (5.2)

> **Rewritten by the keeper at 09:5x UTC to decisions 28 and 29 and to the engine's form B** (the Boss's ruling, comment 6493239195), confirmed by David by name at 10:28 (comment 6494213432) with seven corrections and additions, entered at 10:4x. The tournament of two-part splits along the Meshes, the closure as a token carried along them, "the click is final once the closure has crossed the hand", the closure's cone, the collapse's femtosecond and the state count 5 G + T + 3 + k are the retired reading, kept by name and dated 2026-10-29, 07:0x to 07:4x; their text stands in git.

**Clocks.** A split click decides which part of a spread hand wins: where, which detector, which outcome.
- **The draw is made at the Node-clock where the parts were one** (a pair's birth, a spread quantum's source, a composite's binding), over the products of each part's size on the forward board and on the backward board (decision 29); a silent future, sigma_b alike, makes it Born's rule exactly. For three parts, a composite's, form B is exact with no signal at every timing (David's composite_window.py, 8 of 8).
- **The backward board carries what the click needs back along each twin's own path to that Node-clock,** a detector's frame or the absorbers' takes, merged on the way (David, 10:37), one Mesh a sweep; **the forward board carries the outcome out** along each twin's own path, and the detector's click writes the record. No Node-clock reads past its six neighbours, and nothing crosses space (section 10.1).
- **A pair's parts need no order:** the four parts in any order give one law. **A causal chain takes its order from the forward signal** that makes a later setting depend on an earlier outcome (Animate's loop: one history at all 116 digits when ordered by cause, 40 digits with none when ordered the other way); the cause's order is carried along the forward board, and no Node-clock compares ticks at a distance. The passes verify a history and select none.
- **The draw reads the remainders, which no click can read.** So the choice is set on the board and fair to every reader.
- **A spread quantum's source under decision 40 (b)** (Animate's scheduler, reader-5 c70aa49cf, 16:02, ANIMATE-SCHEDULER and ANIMATE-SCHEMA-1.2; the keeper ran scheduler.py, hand_slot.py and lines.py again with python -I and each output equals its .txt byte for byte; the gate on the head: no refusal, the one INDEX note): a band is one birth's parts, drawn at its birth over sigma_f; no slot joins a band and no slot weighs a history; each slot, once every part that may reach it is drawn, makes its own law-9 draw on its own digit over equal weights, takes as many of its arrivals as it has free and refuses the rest, each back to its giver (5.1's books line); the run's state holds every refusal, so a run back tells a refusal from a take by the state. **Shown, in exact fractions:** the tie (A over x and z, B over y and z, one slot free at z) draws the ends as the Born product (x/y 6/25, x/z 4/25, z/y 9/25, z/z 6/25, the slot splitting z/z into 3/25 with A refused and 3/25 with B refused); A's click at x is 2/5 with B not born and 2/5 with B born; hand_slot's board gives the far twin's + share 1/2 at all four pairs of frames, with the slot and without, the slot refusing the near twin 1/8 of the time and never taking past its free count; parts 1 and 2 of scheduler.txt are equal byte for byte over 400 boards and 9,872 no-signal checks. **For D8, measured:** under (b) A's fate at z moves with B's birth (taken 3/5 with B not born; taken 12/25 and refused 3/25 with B born), which is lawful since z lies on B's birth's light cone, so the cone check reads each click, a take or a refusal, against the settings outside that click's own past cone, and a check that binned a party's whole outcome would name a lawful refusal a signal (ANIMATE-SCHEMA-1.2 part 6). **The record's form** (Animate's proposal, the Boss's to take into schema.py at G5): each birth's split click over sigma_f with no back field (G6's back is dropped, since sigma_b is alike everywhere); the slot's own split click in the one form of every draw; each refusal a Books line at the slot naming its lay. **David's toy:** spread_quantum.py (toys-5 b9615505b, 10:37) weighs sigma_b by the free slots in its pass 2, the weighing that decision 40 (b) withdrew, and its odds of 0 for a full slot with it; he redoes it under (b) with sigma_f alone and the slot refusing after the draw (6502644541); its pass 1 stands as derived, each absorber's share sigma_f being its channel row's fine tooth after the passage, the shares of one quantum summing to one winding, and the redo is to confirm it. The split click's size at an absorber is the whole click's remainder there, so the two clicks close into each other. **Open:** two spread quanta of one train crossing one Node-clock in one span take their passes in turn (the scheduler's).

**Acts** (DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-FAIR-DRAW-AND-POSITION.md and David's 6491076850; the Boss's form B, 6493239195, for stage 9):
- **The number:** d = (c + V) mod L, one carry, where:
  - c is the Node-clock's beat count;
  - V is the sum, over the Node-clock and its six neighbours, of its A1 rows' fine teeth, each read in its row's pitch g (the digit r div g, g the gcd of the row's W and its push's coefficients);
  - L is the sum of the parts' products.
- **The part:** the rank count, part = SUM_i nonneg(d - S_i), with S_i the cumulative products. There is no sort and no if.
- **The law's wording** (the principle's item (5), ALGEBRA.md line 35, as decision 29 has it): the draw is one number d = (c + V) mod L, one carry, and the part is the rank count over the cumulative sizes, each size the product of the part's size on the forward board and on the backward board; the form is the owner's (decision 29, 08:3x). The former wording, a second carry c x the sum of the sizes = t x the draw's range + a remainder, is kept by name and dated 2026-10-29, 08:3x.
- **The size** (David, 10:28): the size of each outcome is the squared whole-number overlap of the forward hand with that outcome's frame, carried by the backward run's tag, a Gaussian norm; it is the product sigma_f sigma_b where the parts are apart; the pair's 2 x 2 determinants are this overlap.
- **On twins against a detector:**
  - sigma_up = (rho rho_d + S . m) / 2 (Fierz's identity, exact on Gaussian integers);
  - L = rho rho_d;
  - down = (2 d - S . m + L) div (2 L), one carry;
  - a pair's joint sizes are the squared whole-number overlaps of one shared hand with the two detectors' frames, a 2 x 2 determinant per pair of outcomes, the frames Gaussian integers (the Boss's joint_from_norms.py);
  - **the hand is forced** (David's shared_hand.py): at a birth alike under the 24 the twins' joint hand is forced, the singlet for two halves and the identity for two vector twins; the twin trains' coupling as an intertwiner of the 24's lifts is owed on the act, with the Boss's probe of the shared hand.
- **What it writes** (Animate, 10:27, item 2): the draw writes nothing, since it is a reading of the state at its beat. The record's Split line is written at the source, where the draw is: its parts are the joint outcomes, each with sigma_f sigma_b (G6 is the sigma_b), and it carries the frames brought back, one per party, and each party's outcome; each detector's click is a whole click (the take) naming that Split line (G5). The winner's windings are books. The backward run writes nothing; the End carries the passes' agreement bit; the header names no switch (decision 33), and a silent tag leaves the record as 1.1's, with back 1.

**Tensors:**
- V is a stencil sum: the own digit plus the six gathered by nb;
- d and the part are elementwise, the part a reduction over the parts' axis;

**New, from the big picture** (derived on paper; probes in preparation):

### The two together (5.3)

- **A detector's event** is a split click, which decides where, then a whole click, which takes the quantum there.
- **Fusion** is a split click, then a whole click. A bound pair runs as one clock, its beat the sum of its parts' (DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-LADDER-AND-FUSION.md).
- **Intense light** at one Node-clock needs no split: its whole clicks take quanta there (way (b)). One quantum spread over the board needs the split first (Grangier).

## 6. Time's cone in the clicks

> **Rewritten by the keeper at 09:5x UTC to decisions 28 and 29 and to form B,** confirmed by David by name at 10:28, with the one addition of item 6. The retired reading's items 3 to 6, the closure's cone as the click's carrier, are kept by name and dated 2026-10-29, 07:0x to 07:4x; their text stands in git.

1. **A whole click is local:** one Node-clock and its six neighbours, in one tick.
2. **The draw is local:** one Node-clock and its six neighbours, in one tick, at the Node-clock where the parts were one.
3. **A spread quantum's click** is final when the draw at its source is made and the word has run out along the quantum's own paths; no cone of the board's own enters it.
4. **Grangier:** one absorber clicks (g2 = 0), because the draw at the source is one draw over the absorbers' sizes carried back.
5. **Bell:** S = -82/29 at every timing of the two clicks, A first, together or B first, with no window; the retired reading gave S = 0 for clicks outside the closure's cone.
6. **Three parts:** exact, with no signal, at every timing on B (David's composite_window.py); one board's closure as written signals in a window (up to 1/4 for GHZ and 4/15 for W), and the one-board forms that do not signal depart from quantum mechanics (by 3/8 and 8/27), as Bancal's theorem says of any finite speed.
7. **The notch:** in a well, or late in the expansion, light's cone narrows (N^2) while the board's reach does not, and the clock there clicks more slowly (the redshift). The zigzag rides the twins' own paths, which the hands keep inside light's cone, so a notch changes the carries' timing and widens no cone.

## 7. The great clock's course: one bang, then the swing

**Clocks:**
- **The bang** is the bottom: the load at 0, every clock at the full rate, and the Mesh at its shortest.
- **The expansion:** the source pushes the count up, and the Mesh lengthens.
- **The top:** the spring overtakes the push, with no trigger and no if.
- **The contraction:** the count falls, and density and heat rise.
- **The bottom:** the source wins again, and that is the next bang. Light pushes twice as hard as matter, so what breaks into light in the heat strengthens it.

**Acts and tensors.** On the board's sum, the grips cancel in pairs: each Mesh's pull is counted from both ends with opposite signs. So the board's summed impulse moves by its summed kick less D times its summed count, exactly. It is one reduction over the board and one driven swing, with no act beside the one act. The period is 2 pi sqrt(Gamma / 2) ticks.

**Measured** (q1 and q1b on 7a's act; DAVID-TENSOR24-THE-COURSE-AND-THE-GROWTH.md):
- 31 tops in 4,300 ticks;
- the period 140.500 against 2 pi sqrt(W / D) = 140.496;
- the tops 1,795 to 1,804, against 2 c* = 1,800;
- the energy kept to the remainders, to 0.01 percent over 214 swings;
- on an 8^3 board, each Node-clock rings with 3 to 22 local tops a swing (median 9), and their own tops part by about a sixth of a swing;
- the run back exact.

**The head's record** (PUSH 37; its README's checks at 9ca2b985b):
- three cycles in 2,400 intervals, each higher than the last: U at the tops 1.80, then 2.17, then 2.48 and rising, with the falls 1.8035 to 1.7765 and 2.1659 to 2.1465;
- the second bang keeps all the information: 12 tracks cross the return.

**Status:**
- OPEN 7 and item (w) close by the spring (the owner's word of 06:08 UTC: no trigger at the top);
- the fifth act's reversal at the top is cancelled, not being a local law (the owner's word to the Boss of 06:3x UTC, decision 27). Sofer cancelled **The turnover** and its second trigger **The floor** in place on that word (ALGEBRA.md lines 1602 and 1608, scribe-5 ad8103091): the tick has four acts. T = J K stays each kind's time reversal, read by the tests and applied by no act;
- as the clicks see 7b's swing (the time gear on its own teeth) the expansion accelerates, q near -2, against nature's +1/2 to -0.55 (David, 10:37; section 12.3); decision 36 (12:12) puts the time gear under the shared ruler, no road C: derived in toys, open by name and owed on the act, and no contradiction booked;
- **under road (II) the free-count tie makes the lapse rise as a^3 in the course** (David, 12.3 item 21): the bang then has the slowest clocks and the top runs at the full rate, which reverses the bang's description above; **decision 39 (13:4x) makes (II) the form**, so the swing is derived in toys under A and owed under the free count (David, 12.3 items 21 and 24; the Boss's plan: "the swing under the free count" is David's), and the descriptions of the bang and the top above stand as road A's until it is run.

## 8. Three times, and what the clicks see

**Three times:**
1. **The great clock's time gear** (the load) swings. This is what turns in the head's record.
2. **Each Node-clock's tick** always goes forward. The notch changes its units along with everything else.
3. **The arrow, the record's order,** always grows.

The way back stands in the law, in the remainders. No act of the board runs it: the spring turns the swing, and the act at the top is cancelled (decision 27). The runner's rewind check runs it at every record's end (runner/rewind.py: every round undone from its out, the last first, back to the seed, the End line writing the rounds undone and whether the state came back exact); it is a check and no act of the board, and the record stands through it (Animate's precision, 6490628536).

**What the clicks see** (**The same clock and the same ruler for every act**; **The great clock's tick is no observable**):
- **Where they stand, nothing.** A uniform notch is a change of units: every clock, ruler and bound body changes together.
- **Through light from another time, with a delay.** The redshift compares the load at the emission with the load at the reception.
  - After the top, the blue arrives first from the nearest.
  - The far light, sent during the expansion, keeps arriving red.
  - So in the contraction, the clicks still see expansion, for a long time.
- **Each return looks like a new bang.** The head's decoder, which reads clicks alone, named the two returns "bang 2" and "bang 3".
- **In the board's ticks, which no click reads,** the clicks are sparsest at the top and densest at the bottom (the head's check 5: the spacing follows e^U on both sides of both returns).
  - That is the owner's "the board returns when the clicks weaken", read the other way round.
  - The swing sets both, and the board reads no click.

### What time is, and what the clicks are: the block and its shadow (8.1)

The owner's word to the Boss of 11:30 (comment 6495446181; in Hebrew; in translation): "The solution may also need to come from the dimensions. The clicks see a three-dimensional space, and in fact we are in a four-dimensional space, no? With the boards: with the boards and the clicks we are in four dimensions, and the clicks see three. And it must all be seen from one point, so the clicks are a kind of shadow, right? The clicks are a shadow of the four-dimensional space. Search in Minkowski; maybe the solution goes through Minkowski's idea. And what is time, really?" And of 11:33 (comment 6495522401): "If these are general definitions that are also correct, pass them to Sofer, so that we put them into the overall picture too." The Boss answered the first in items 1 and 2 of his comment, put the answers to the law, and sent four definitions and one direction; the keeper checked each against the law and enters them with the status the Boss gives, adding the care where the law asks one.

1. **D1, the block** (derived from decisions 28 and 34; a reading of the construction, with no term added). The history that agrees with itself, the one hand run forward and back, is a four-dimensional block of the board: its three axes and the tick. **The care (the keeper's):** the block is a reading of the history, the record the forward run writes and the tag the backward run carries, and not an array the engine holds; the engine holds the Node-clock's state alone (state only in the Node-clock).
2. **D2, the clicks as a shadow** (derived). A click at a Node-clock sees the block from one point: its own path and the light that arrives along its past cone, which it takes (the record holds takes and no light: the owner's word of 14:4x, 13.2 item F3). So the clicks see a 3 + 1 shadow of the block: space as light brings it, and their own time. It agrees with section 8 above ("through light from another time") and with **The great clock's tick is no observable** (ALGEBRA.md line 695).
3. **D3, the faces of time** (derived; section 8's three times, with the tick's two directions of decision 34 added): **the tick,** the block's fourth axis, the order in which the act steps, with two directions in the run that meet at the split click; **the great clock's load,** the time of the scale, which swings; **a clock's own beats,** its proper time, which the notch slows by N and motion slows too, the clicks reading it and never reading the board's tick (**Einstein lives in the clicks of bound bobs, and the great clock is never measured**, ALGEBRA.md line 1507); **the record's order,** the arrow, forward because the forward run alone writes.

Light's speed sets the cone, one for every train and for gravity: s grip / W = 1/6 for every row at 7b (the Boss's second check, 13.1), light at 1/sqrt 6 Mesh a tick at the full rate and c0 N^2 at a notch; along a path, Minkowski's interval is its clock's own count in the long-wave limit. **The care (the law's own, lines 1507, 1539 and 1554):** at the Mesh's scale the board keeps its frame: the symmetry is the band's at long wavelength and breaks at the lattice's own order k^2 / 12, so Lorentz invariance is not exact at the Mesh's scale (the Boss's plan names it as its root II). **Whether that frame shows in the shadow is open** (the owner's word of 11:35, item 6): the Boss asks that it not be entered as derived until the causal-set test closes it, and root II reads, in the Boss's wording of 12:40 (6496932945, item 2(b)), "the board's frame at the Mesh's scale; in the shadow it fades as the clicks thin (as sqrt p or p), except toward the board's own null directions. At light's 1/sqrt 6 the 3 + 1 board has 24 null vectors at t = 6, and there a boosted observer sees the frame past a rapidity of about (1/4) ln(C/p). Measured on a thinned board, not on the record."
5. **D5, open: the 4 in X = e^(4U) is 3 + 1** (a direction and no definition; the Boss's two probes running, the two-gear test and the fresh attempt). The great clock may count the board's four-dimensional measure, linear in X, while each clock's rate composes in one dimension (Postulate 6); the course the clicks need would then be that law's shadow from one point. It is the same door as David's volume form and the Boss's candidate of 12.3, item 11, seen from Minkowski, and it enters as open until the probes or David close it; what must still hold: the wells add at first order, as in nature where they add only at first order, gamma = 1, and nature's q per era.

6. **The owner's rule of 11:35 and the causal-set mechanism** (the Boss, comment 6495563410; the owner's word to him: "See how R is settled by the same rule of the dimensions and the shadow. Maybe all the parameters you talk about are not parameters of the shadow. Sorkin; Bombelli, Henson and Sorkin, 2009, on the Lorentz invariance of a sprinkling): a set of events with only their causal order and their count gives back Minkowski's geometry, the order the cone and the number in a region its four-volume, and events spread as a random sprinkling keep Lorentz invariance exactly in distribution though any regular grid shows a frame. **The clicks are such events:** their causal order is the record's order and their number per region is a four-volume, the measure of D5's X, which ties the two open items together; it fails where the counts are equal while the paths differ and where they differ by other than the lapse and the motion (guards G5 and G7 or G1); owed on a record that holds two clocks meeting twice. **(4) The cone check on every record:** for every party, its outcome's law against every setting outside its past cone, binned by that setting; equal laws within the Poisson spread, or the record is named as signalling with the pair named; under the slot fix ((b) of the Boss's 6499432823) it holds on hand_slot's two boards exactly, and under "a full slot weighs 0" it fails there (11/29 against 107/174;
