## 9. Does the whole universe reverse time together? No

- **What turns is the scale, not the arrow.** The gears go on forward, and the record grows through every top.
- **There is no shared moment.** Each Node-clock has 3 to 22 local tops a swing. The one top is the board's mean's, and no Node-clock reads it.
- **A reversal of every gear at the top** would be an act on the whole board, against **Locality is never broken**. That was the fifth act's reversal, and the owner cancelled it at 06:3x UTC as no local law (decision 27).
  - So no act turns the whole universe together.
  - Had it stood, every cycle would have replayed the last backward, until the clicks broke the mirror.
- **Even then no click could tell.** The act is symmetric in time (T S T = S^-1), and for the clicks, time is the record's order.

### Where time can go back, and where the board goes forward (9.1)

The owner's word to David of 07:18 UTC (the list above) asks when the board's time can go back and when it goes forward, with the two kinds of clicks and the remainders, and what it gives that light moves at 1 / sqrt 6. David's answer (6490839743, item 6), each line with its status word, entered by the keeper:

1. **Where time can go back** (derived). The act is symmetric in time (T S T = S^-1), so where there are no clicks forward and back cannot be told apart: a contraction there is the mirrored board's expansion run back. There the great clock's time, the load, turns back at the top by the spring alone, with no act (decision 27); what swings is the load, and each clock's beat count only grows (the first of the three times of section 8).
2. **Where the board goes forward** (derived; one step open). A click writes a record that the state alone cannot undo: its bits, the free slots and the take's write (the click page's mutants), so the arrow is the direction in which the record grows. Where the records sit in composites, their own later clicks read them (a machine acts by what it recorded), so the arrow feeds itself where clicks are linked to more complex clocks (read with section 11.1: the feeding is the record's crossing the bang, link 4; a lean of the draws by the backward board is link 8, not claimed). The open step: how strongly such a record locks the direction, against one that disperses.
3. **The two clicks and the remainders** (derived). The remainders are the memory and the way back. The whole click builds, laying a hand in a slot. The split click chooses from the past's remainders, so it sets the direction of cause, and the draw does not read alike under the mirror.
4. **The click's order** (derived on paper). A click is final when the draw at its source is made and the outcomes have run out along the twins; the order of two clicks is the cause's order, carried along the forward board where a later setting depends on an earlier outcome, and a spacelike pair needs none (the four parts in any order give one law, Animate's toy). So the board's time order of clicks is the forward signal's, and no cone of the click's own enters; either click may come first (David, 10:28). The retired reading, kept by name and dated: the closure's cone ordering pairs that light cannot order, with the window where l5 found the three-party signal.
5. **The picture, a mechanical clock** (a reading): the swing is the pendulum, the clicks are the escapement and the composites are the gear train; in a clock the arrow is in the escapement, not in the pendulum.
6. **What light at 1 / sqrt 6 gives** (derived): one species per particle, by (g)'s hold; the board's own number, 1/6 being one over the six Spokes, so that no constant is typed; one cone for light and gravity, as in nature; and the swing's period of exactly 2 pi times light's crossing of gravity's reach (the reach sqrt(Gamma / 12) Meshes, crossed in sqrt(Gamma / 2) ticks). The retired reading added the room between the click's cone and light's, where the closure ran ahead of light.
7. **A test owed:** one board at the top of the swing, run with clicks and without, to read whether the contraction mirrors the expansion.
8. **Can a Node-clock's two clocks turn their direction, together or each on its own?** (the owner's question to the Boss, relayed at 10:12, comment 6493891638; the Boss's answer, derived on paper): **each on its own: no**, since two clocks of one direction at a Node-clock cannot meet (the draw would read the forward size twice, sigma_f squared, not Born) and the record would have two writers or none; **together at one Node-clock:** where no click has been written the swap changes nothing any click can read (point 1: T S T = S^-1), and where a click has been written the record fixes which clock runs forward, the one in whose direction the record grows, so the clicks set the direction and nothing switches it; **together on the whole board at once** is the reversal at the top, cancelled as no local law (decision 27). What does turn is the load, the great clock's swing, alike on both clocks: the forward clock runs it forward and the backward clock runs the same history back (10.7).

**Status:** derived as above (points 1 to 4, 6 and 8; points 4 and 6 rewritten by the keeper at 09:5x to decision 28, confirmed by David at 10:28; point 8 the Boss's, entered at 10:2x), a reading (point 5), a direction (point 2's open step); the probe of point 7 owed. Kept by Sofer: nothing here reverses the board, so section 9's answer, no, stands; what turns back is the load.

## 10. Bell at a distance that is not at a distance

> **Decided by the owner's word of 07:44 (decision 28): Bell at a distance stands on two boards on one Node-clock (10.1 to 10.4), seen since the owner's words of 10:10 and 10:38 (decisions 31 and 34) as one Node-clock with one hand run both ways (10.6 and 10.7).** This section was rewritten by the keeper at 09:5x UTC to keep what stands and to name what is retired; David confirmed it by name at 10:28, with the shared hand and the window check added. The older text stands in git.

- **Against nature (kept).** The Bell tests at a distance (Aspect; Weihs; Delft; Micius) keep the correlation even where an influence would need at least about ten thousand times light's speed (Salart and others, 2008; Yin and others, 2013; the loophole-free tests of 2015 at 1.3 km and the satellite pair of 2017 at 1,200 km). On two boards no influence crosses space at any speed, so these tests are met with no bound on a speed.
- **The theorem (kept).** Under the generic Node-clock |S| is at most 2 for every rule of the engine where the settings are free of the pair, late or random (**Tsirelson's bound is a theorem of the credit's form**); Animate's one forward board confirms it on a toy, exhausted. The finding against nature by name stands for the engine-5.0 line, which holds no backward board, and is lifted for Tensor24, whose every Node-clock runs both ways (decisions 33 and 34), when the probes run on the engine (ALGEBRA.md lines 92 and 1173). **The twins' shared hand is the 24's** (David's shared_hand.py): at a birth alike under the 24 the twins' joint hand is the one A1 line of two halves, the singlet; entanglement is that hand, and its click is the zigzag.
- **Retired by decision 28, kept by name and dated 2026-10-29, 07:0x to 07:4x:**
  - the closure across space at the board's cone as Bell's carrier, with its window (two clicks within D / (sqrt 6 e^(2U) c) of each other falling outside the cone and giving S = 0), its three-party signal (l5) and Bancal's catch (Bancal, Pironio, Acin, Liang, Scarani and Gisin, Nature Physics 8, 867, 2012; a three-party form by Barnea and others, 2013: an influence of any finite speed, within its window, either signals in some setups of several parties or departs from the quantum predictions there); David's composite_window.py checks Bancal's catch on three forms of the closure, and each signals or departs in a window of three parts;
  - the cone's width as e^(2U) / c0 times light's, with its one cost Gamma and its one condition that the closure reads no ruler;
  - item (l), the pair clicking as one clock wherever its parts are, and the ways 1 to 3 that were put to the owner (the closure at the reach, a wide cone by a constant at load 0, item (l)); way 4, two boards on one Node-clock, was his choice;
  - their measures (g2 = 0 with A 1,437 and B 1,513; S = -2.86 inside the cone and 0 in the window), taken under the closure and owed again on two boards; light at c0 N^2 to six digits at the counts 0, 100, 1,000 and 3,000 (l4 on 7b's gearing) stays measured.
- **The law's line.** **Locality is never broken** (ALGEBRA.md line 92) holds, with its sentence rewritten by decision 28 (lines 35, 92, 1173 and 1934): the correlation of a pair runs through its shared birth on the two boards, every read within six neighbours; the sweeps' locality (reading 7) stays open for a full board, and the schedule that meets it is form B's three passes (10.1, Animate's toy).
- **Status:** decided (the owner's word of 07:44); the substance is 10.1 to 10.4.

### Two boards on one Node-clock, one forward and one backward: decided (10.1)

Decided by the owner's word to the Boss of 2026-10-29, 07:44 UTC ("The two boards.", decision 28, #2187 comment 6490979060; the law's clause is ALGEBRA.md line 35). Entered by the Boss, while it was an option, on the owner's words to the Boss of 2026-10-29, 07:2x to 07:4x UTC (in Hebrew; in translation): "And if there are two boards running on one node, one backward and one forward, can locality be kept? Because locality must not be broken." Then: "Put it as an option in the big picture file, and send it to David too." Then: "What does each board stand for, and how do the clicks enter, with the two kinds?" And: "It supports making more complex clocks, because that is the universe's aim."

- **Seen as one Node-clock with one hand run both ways** (decision 31, the owner's word of 10:10: "Better to see it as one board with doubled Node-clocks, which simulates a double board, no? Let the clicks be derived exactly from the full picture."; decision 34, 10:38: "The picture closes without a flag. It is not really a doubled Node-clock, is it? Because there is one hand..?"): the forward board of this section is the forward run of the hand and the backward board is the backward run, the same hand run back, carrying a tag; one geometry, one set of Meshes, one great clock; each run reads within the six neighbours (10.6). The "two dials" of the lines below are the forward run and the backward run's tag. Whether the two runs can turn their direction, together or each on its own, is 9.1, point 8: no, the clicks set the direction.
- **What each board stands for:**
  - **the forward board is the past arriving:** what was prepared (the bang, the sources, the pair's birth); it carries the waves forward (light, matter, gravity) and it alone writes records, the quotient leaving forward; it is the board as built;
  - **the backward board is the future arriving backward:** what the detectors will choose and click; it is the same act run back, carries the choices back, and writes no record, so the clicks never see it, only the correlations it makes;
  - **the present, at every Node-clock,** is where the two dials meet.
- **How the two clicks enter:**
  - **the whole click (how much)** stands on the forward board as built: the drive at the detector reaches a whole winding (q at least 1) and the record is written; the backward board takes from it only the confirmation, one quantum taken, back to the source, so the giver and the taker are the two ends of one handshake and the books close at both;
  - **the split click (which)** is where the backward board works: detector A's choice and outcome ride back along A's twin to the birth, and the other twin's forward dial brings them to B; B's draw reads its forward dial, which already holds A's choice, and Bell follows;
  - **one spread quantum (Grangier)** the same way: once a part is drawn "here", the word runs back to the source and out to every other part, so no other detector clicks (g2 = 0), with no closure across space.

- **In clocks:**
  - every Node-clock has one hand, run forward from the bang and back from the clicks to come (decision 34), and a twin carries the hand forward and the tag back;
  - when detector A chooses and clicks, its choice rides the backward dial back along its twin's own path to the birth's Node-clock, one Mesh at a time;
  - there it passes to the other twin's forward dial and rides with it to B, one Mesh at a time;
  - no step reads past a Node-clock's six neighbours, and nothing crosses space at any speed: the correlation travels through the shared birth, a zigzag in time.
- **In the act:** the board's step runs back exactly (the untick), so the backward board is the same act run back, with no new term.
- **In physics:** Aharonov's two-state vector (Aharonov, Bergmann and Lebowitz, 1964; Aharonov and Vaidman), Cramer's transactional reading (1986), and the locally mediated reformulations that Wharton and Argaman review (Reviews of Modern Physics 92, 021002, 2020). Locality is kept; what Bell's theorem loses is the independence of the source from the settings to come, through the future and not through a shared past.
- **What it would lift:** the window of section 10 and Bancal's catch. No influence crosses space at any finite speed, so no setup of several parties falls inside a window, and the open step of l5 does not arise.
- **What is open, each with a probe that can fail:**
  1. **Consistency.** The backward board starts from the clicks, and the clicks read it, so the whole history must agree with itself at both ends. The runner then searches for that history and cannot only run forward tick by tick; whether one always exists, and only one, is open.
  2. **No signal into the past.** Each end's statistics must not depend on the other end's choice, in every setup of two and of three parties.
  3. **The cost.** Two states at every Node-clock, and the search.
  4. **The aim kept.** The search for a history that agrees with itself must not forbid composite clocks.
- **The aim, ever more complex clocks** (the owner's reason for the option):
  - a composite clock stays one clock: parts born together stay correlated at any distance until their first click, with no read beyond the neighbours, which is what pairs, atoms, molecules and machines need;
  - the arrow is kept: the forward board alone writes records and the backward board neither writes nor erases, so the record grows from swing to swing and every bang shows more;
  - **retired at 10:2x, kept by name and dated** (David's comment 6494213432 item 4 and his page, section 6; the Boss's ask 6492236807 item 3; the owner's "Born.", 10:0x): a lean of the draws toward complex futures. A history that agrees with itself gives the forward law exactly, P(history) = P(future) P(history | future); the act's own count of paths, as the future's weight, gives the toy no steady lean (the Boss's reading (ii)); the owner declared no weight on the future; the lean's weight from the act: there is none. In its place: the two boards keep the forward ratchet (11.1, link 6) and give a composite's parts one correlation at any distance (link 7); the aim's engine is the forward ratchet (11.2).
- **The probe:** a small board, a pair, two detectors that choose late, both boards, the search; S, the marginals of two and of three parties, and whether a consistent history exists.
- **David's derivation of the split click on two boards** (6491076850, the owner's word to David of 07:46, "The subtle point is the solution... the only aim is complex clocks that create interesting clicks"; entered by the keeper):
  - **The subtle point.** On a deterministic board the backward run from the end is only the forward run reversed, so it adds nothing; for two boards to add anything, the split click's outcome must be fixed where the two dials meet, not by the forward draw alone.
  - **The rule: the split click draws by the product of the two dials' sizes** (derived on paper, the probe owed; approved by the owner, decision 29, 08:3x, and entered in the principle's item (5), ALGEBRA.md line 35).
    - In clocks: every Node-clock holds two dials; the forward dial turns from the bang and carries the past's sizes sigma_f; the backward dial turns from the clicks to come and carries the future's sizes sigma_b, one Mesh a tick backward; the split click is where they meet, and the tick cancels in the product of the two (8.1 item 13).
    - In acts: L = SUM_i sigma_f,i sigma_b,i; d = (c + V) mod L, one carry; part = SUM_i nonneg(d - S_i), with S_i the cumulative products. Each part is drawn exactly sigma_f,i sigma_b,i times over any L consecutive beats; the fair draw's proof is unchanged. When the future is silent (sigma_b alike for every part) this is law 9 as it is, exactly: Born. So the fair draw is the case of a silent future.
    - In physics: the rule of Aharonov, Bergmann and Lebowitz (1964): the odds of an outcome are its weight from the past times its weight from the future.
    - In tensors: the backward dial's sizes are one more set of integers per Node-clock, carried by the untick's gather along nb; the draw stays elementwise, the rank count a reduction over the parts.
    - The whole click (how much) is decided by the forward board alone, as built, and writes the record; the backward board writes none, so the arrow stays the record's growth.
  - **Bell by the zigzag** (derived on paper; the probe owed): the backward board carries A's outcome, its axis a and its sign s, back along A's twin to the birth, and the birth's forward dial carries it out along B's twin; sigma_f from the birth is alike for B's two outcomes and sigma_b comes from A's outcome, the twins' Fierz sizes of B's detector against -s a, (1 -/+ s a . b) / 2 in integers; B's draw over the products gives P(B = +/- | A = s) = (1 -/+ s a . b) / 2, so E = -a . b and S = -82/29 at the plan's axes with every marginal 1/2; conditioning A on B or B on A gives the same joint law, so the act may fix the order by the board's tick, ties broken by a rule the 24 leave alike; no influence crosses space at any speed, so there is no window and Bancal's catch does not arise.
  - **The aim** (retired, as the line above; the text stands in git): (David's comment 6494213432 item 4 and his page, section 6; the Boss's ask 6492236807 item 3; the owner's "Born.", 10:0x): a lean of the draws toward complex futures. A history that agrees with itself gives the forward law exactly, P(history) = P(future) P(history | future); the act's own count of paths, as the future's weight, gives the toy no steady lean (the Boss's reading (ii)); the owner declared no weight on the future; the lean's weight from the act: there is none. In its place: the two boards keep the forward ratchet (11.1, link 6) and give a composite's parts one correlation at any distance (link 7); the aim's engine is the forward ratchet (11.2).
  - **What stays open, each with its probe:** the history must agree with itself at both ends (the runner sweeps forward and back, each sweep local with every read within six neighbours, until the two runs agree at every Node-clock; open: whether such a history exists, whether it is unique, whether loops close); no signal into the past (the marginals of two and of three parties must not depend on any other party's setting); the cost (the backward tag's integers per Node-clock, at most 6 T, and the sweeps); the law (the product draw is the owner's by decision 29 and stands in the principle's item (5), ALGEBRA.md line 35; line 92's sentence was rewritten by decision 28). The probe is the Boss's: a small board, a pair, two late choosers, both boards and the sweeps; S, the marginals of two and of three parties, the count of consistent histories, and a loop case.
- **David's probe, shown in exact integers** (comment 6492229306, the owner's words to David of 08:25, "What do two boards give nature? Does it give complex clocks by the clicks' choice?", and 08:28, "Show it."; toys-5 f3661f524, tools/derivations/open_items/derive/david_open/two_boards/two_boards.py; the keeper ran it again with python -I and its output equals two_boards.txt byte for byte; the push adds three files under derive/david_open and no engine file), read by the keeper, line by line:
  1. **The product draw is exact** (derived, shown): each part drawn exactly sigma_f sigma_b times over a full turn in 67,104 of 67,104 cases (1 to 4 parts, sizes 0 to 3), and a silent future draws by sigma_f alone, law 9's draw, Born: 1,008 of 1,008 cases.
  2. **Bell by the zigzag** (derived on paper, shown beside the engine): S = -82/29 at three timings of the two clicks (A first, both at one tick, B first), E = -21/29, -21/29, -20/29 and 20/29 at the plan's four settings, every marginal 1/2, where the closure at the board's cone read S = 0 at one tick (l4). **What the probe checks, and what it does not:** the page puts the birth's joint sizes in as the singlet's odds, 29 - s_A s_B k over 116 for the plan's four axes, so what is shown is that the bookkeeping of the zigzag (both settings carried back to the birth, one joint draw there, both outcomes carried out) gives S = -82/29 and the marginals at every timing, exactly. The joint sizes' derivation from the twins' frames in the act is the engine probe's (stage 9). **The Boss's joint_from_norms.py** (tensor24-review 70ea1991b, 09:31; the keeper ran it again with python -I, its output equal to joint_from_norms.txt byte for byte) derives them beside the engine: the joint size of each pair of outcomes is the square of the overlap of one shared hand, psi = e1 x e2 - e2 x e1 (total zero), with the two detectors' frames, a whole number, so that the sizes (4, 25, 25, 4) at two settings, (9, 49, 49, 9) and (49, 9, 9, 49) equal David's over 116 at all four settings, E = -21/29, -21/29, -20/29 and 20/29, S = -82/29, nothing typed but the four frames (Gaussian integers); twins with separate hands, a product state, give at most 42/29 over 36 pairs of hands, an illustration of Bell's bound of 2 and no proof of it. What stays owed is the engine's own probe: the same sizes from the twins' frames in the act's arrays, and GHZ's. Its counts "464 of 464 histories agree at both ends" and "reads across space: 0" are by construction (the first counter adds one per draw, the second is never added to), so they are no evidence; the one real check on locality in the code is the assertion that every carry moves at most one Mesh in one step along a twin's worldline.

### One board or two (10.4)

The owner's word to the Boss of 08:59 asks whether we need to return to one board. **Decided by the owner:** two boards (his word to Animate of 09:5x, "The influence from the future to the past is necessary.", and directly to Sofer at 10:0x, "We chose two boards, enter everything into them... and show a closed picture."). The facts, each with its place in the law:
- **No switch** (decision 33): two boards always; the one board is the old theory's, kept as the control of a test of the engine (a tag held silent), and decision 29's whether of the universe file is retired (10.2).
- **What one board gives of Bell at a distance, as the law stands:** with the backward board off no train carries the detectors' frames to the fitting, so the finding against nature by name stands for that universe (ALGEBRA.md lines 92 and 1173); every forward-only history gives S at most 2 in size (Animate's F, exhausted).
- **What a return to the closure at the board's cone would be:** the Boss's one board's Bell (6492382830, item 1.4). Decision 28 retired that closure as Bell's carrier, with its window and Bancal's catch, and item (l), kept by name and dated; to take it up again needs the owner's word to undo that retirement, and its open step, the form inside a window of several parties where l5 measured a signal in the form as written, reopens with the keeper's reading 6 (the closure is an act between two Node-clocks).
- **What two boards give, and what is held to open steps:** no window and no Bancal catch; the zigzag's S, three parties and Grangier shown beside the engine. Held to: the joint sizes from the act, loops beyond one step, the scheduler on a full board, whether each sweep is local (reading 7), and the order (reading 15).
- **The Boss's recommendation** (6492511185, item 2, which revises item 2 of 6492382830): two boards for the coherent picture of nature, with Born in the band; one board stays the switch off, for runs that need no Bell at a distance (retired by decision 33). Complexity does not decide it: the two boards add nothing to it (11.1, link 8; 11.3).
- **The owner's question to David** (09:0x, "Does this need two boards?"): David, for complex clocks no and for Bell with no window yes; the Boss, Animate and the keeper agree (11.3, item 8).
- **Which converges more nicely** (the owner's word to the Boss of 09:39, comment 6493239195: "Let's try to get a closed picture of everything... and what converges more nicely, one board or two boards..."; the Boss's argument, and the keeper agrees): **one board, as the law stands, does not close nature:** Bell at a distance stays at most 2 and two absorbers of one quantum click together with odds 1/4 (Animate's F, exhausted); to close them it needs the closure that decision 28 retired, whose window is where Bancal's theorem leaves only a signal or a departure from the quantum law (l5 measured a signal). **Two boards give every row of 10.3 one local mechanism:** the backward board is the act run back (the untick, exact), the draw is law 9's over the products (Born where the future is silent), the pair's odds are Gaussian norms of one shared hand, and every influence rides along the twins' own paths, with no window. **Complexity does not decide it:** the forward ratchet is the same on both. **The one rule two boards need is the cause's order (B), and it is local;** without it a fair choice passes Tsirelson's bound (Animate's U). **The cost:** k_b integers per Node-clock and the runner's three passes (forward to the settings, back along the twins to the birth, forward with the outcomes), which verify a history and select none. **The keeper's verdict: two boards;** the owner decided two boards at 10:0x, and decision 33 retired the switch, so two boards always.
- **The owner's word to Animate** (09:5x, her comment 6493467910): after "Do you think one board is enough?" and "Isn't that non-local and forbidden?" (on the engine computing a whole history in passes back and forth, not only tick after tick), "The influence from the future to the past is necessary." **Animate's reading** (the Boss to confirm with the owner, the keeper to enter in the law if he does): it settles this section for the picture of nature, the backward board being the one carrier of an influence from the future to the past, so the picture is two boards (form B); the switch off stays a run that leaves Bell at a distance and Grangier out, by name (retired by decision 33); the line stays, locality in space (every read within six neighbours) and no selection by the whole history. **The keeper agrees with the reading and enters it here, not yet in the law** (reading 20).
- **The owner's word to the Boss** (10:0x, relayed at 10:06, decision 30, comment 6493783603; in Hebrew; in translation): "4: the clicks lengthen the future forward. So one board or two, in your view? We chose two boards: bring everything into them... and show a closed picture. In the code the Node-clock is to be doubled so as not to duplicate code, no? Because they do similar acts... and maybe it is really two nodes in nature?" The Boss's answer to his "in your view": two boards, as in his 6493239195; the switch of decision 29 stood then, and decision 33 retired it at 10:37. The rest of that word, the Node-clock doubled in the code and "maybe it is really two nodes in nature", is 10.6.
- **Read in decisions 31 and 34** (10:10 and 10:38): "one board" in this section is the forward run alone, the old theory's; "two boards" is the forward run and the backward run of the one hand.
- **The keeper's reading:** the law holds two boards always (decision 33); a return to one board would need the closure at the board's cone, which decision 28 retired, and the owner's word.
- **Status:** decided: two boards (the owner's word of 10:0x, directly to Sofer and to the Boss), with no switch (decision 33); the owner's question of 08:59 answered.

### Why nature has two boards on a Node-clock: Animate's reading (10.5)

The owner's question to Animate (09:5x; her comment 6493669974; in Hebrew; in translation): "Why, in your opinion, did nature choose two boards on a Node-clock?" Then his word: "Pass the explanation to everyone and to the full picture." Her answer (ANIMATE-TWO-BOARDS, part 2b, reader-5 e4a68c8b6; the keeper read it against the law, nothing crosses), each line with its status word:

**In one line (a reading):** the law runs both ways, and only where the future meets the past at the source can a click be whole without anything crossing space.
1. **The law itself runs both ways** (measured): the one act runs back exactly, the untick, and the run back is exact in every record; a law that runs back has no direction of its own, and the backward board is the same act read the other way. What runs one way is the records, which the forward board alone writes (decision 28): the law is symmetric and the world has an arrow.
2. **A click must be whole and local at once** (derived): one quantum spread over several absorbers, or two twins far apart, need one outcome kept together while every act reads its six neighbours alone. A forward board alone cannot do it: Bell's S is at most 2, and two absorbers click together with odds 1/4 (Animate's toy). The parts are kept together either across space (the closure faster than light, with its window and its three-party step) or across time, through the source where the parts were one: the future's settings ride back along each part's own path and the outcome rides out along the same path. **Across time is the cheaper way** (a reading): no new speed, no window, no preferred frame.
3. **A click is a meeting** (the owner's law of 2026-10-28, ALGEBRA.md line 1021): the source offers and the absorber confirms, and one board holds the offer with no one to take it. Three precedents in physics: Wheeler and Feynman (1945), waves from the absorber, from the future, to explain radiation; Aharonov, Bergmann and Lebowitz (1964), two state vectors; Cramer (1986), each event a handshake between emitter and absorber.
4. **It costs nothing else** (derived): a history that agrees with itself draws by the forward law, Born (P(history) = P(future) P(history | future), the Boss's identity), so the backward board disturbs nothing else, the arrow stays the records' and complexity grows by the forward ratchet; the one addition is the tag the backward run carries.
5. **In clocks** (a direction): each Node-clock has one hand, run forward from the bang and back from the clicks to come; the present is where the runs meet, and a click is that meeting. The owner's thought of 10:0x, that a node is in fact two, and his word of 10:38, that there is one hand, are this.
6. **A correction** (Animate's 6493694942, on the owner's word to her, "Why should a choice not be needed? Maybe that is the click."): the line "no selection is ever needed on a full board" was put too broadly, and it is replaced here. **The click is a choice.** Under B the pair's four joint outcomes are four histories, and the one law-9 draw at the birth chooses one of them, made at one Node-clock by its own digits (a reading). What stays forbidden is a choice that no single Node-clock can make, one that compares whole histories across the board (reading 7). The condition, measured on her toy: the choice must be made where the histories part, the source, and over the right sizes, the products; a fair coin between two finished histories gives S = -106/29, past nature's bound, and the same choice made as the click at the birth gives -82/29. The owner's one question of section 14, where two histories agree which one is kept, then reads: the click decides, by its own draw over the products, with no rule beside it; where the future's weight is the history's own that is Born, and a lean needs a typed weight, which item (9) refuses. The full-board step, restated: every band of agreeing histories has a Node-clock where they part, so one click there decides it; a band with none would need a choice by the whole board, which is no click, and that is what the scheduler's probe must look for.
**What does not follow:** that nature "chose" (the clicks can only show that, among the local mechanisms, two boards is the one that fits the clicks nature shows); that every band of agreeing histories on a full board has a Node-clock where it parts (the scheduler, open).

**Status:** a reading and derived as marked; entered at the owner's word to pass the explanation to the full picture.

### One hand run both ways, the tag, and two nodes in nature: the Boss's design (10.6)

The owner's word to the Boss of 10:0x UTC (decision 30, relayed at 10:06, comment 6493783603; in Hebrew; in translation): "4: the clicks lengthen the future forward. So one board or two, in your view? We chose two boards: bring everything into them... and show a closed picture. In the code the Node-clock is to be doubled so as not to duplicate code, no? Because they do similar acts... and maybe it is really two nodes in nature?" The first two sentences are decided and entered (12.1 and 10.4; the law, line 35). The last two are his questions, and the Boss answered them with a design (tensor24-review 9151a155c, the plan's decision 30 (ad)). In the same hour he said directly to the keeper: "The implementation has to think about whether a node is in fact two... and that simulates two boards." (ALGEBRA.md line 35, **The implementation**). Each line with its status word:

1. **One code for both boards** (the Boss's design of 10:0x, decision 30; **refined by him at 10:12, section 10.7**): first stated as one more axis of two on each state array, the forward half stepping by tick and the backward half by untick, the same act run back (the Boss: both built and exact), the split click reading the product of the two halves' sizes along that axis (the draw of 5.2) and the 24 acting on both halves alike. Refined: only the backward dial is doubled, since in a history that agrees with itself the backward clock's gears retrace the forward clock's tick for tick and need no arrays of their own; the first statement is kept here by name. **Read since decision 34** (10:38): there is one hand, and what the backward run carries is a tag, not a hand.
2. **The T check is in** (the Boss, comment 6494224028; tensor24-review 9a7db7432, derive/boss/tsym/tsym.py and .txt, about 16 seconds; the keeper ran it again with python -I and the output equals the committed text byte for byte). T is derived carry by carry from engine/step.py: a and b exchanged on every row, the gear's tooth r -> W - 1 - r at the W the state's own next tick reads, the going go -> 1 - go, the drive's tooth rd -> T_d - 1 - rd, the dial ph -> Gamma - 1 - ph, the count and the time c -> C - c and tn -> C - tn.
   - **Exact (measured on the act):** tick(T s1) = T s0 on all eight components and the out at 720 states of three universes (light, matter, both); T T = 1; the doubled clock, 120 ticks forward, T, 120 forward, T, lands on the laid state in all six runs; the due beats reverse; each part is needed (r flipped at the laid W and rd left unflipped fail 40 of 40; go + 1 passes a round and is no involution).
   - **Where it does not hold, and what it means for the build:** with staggered counts (a run under masks) the state does not hold which sites fired, and the backward clock takes the forward fire from the log as its mask and is exact again in 31 of 31 cases (the runner keeps the outs, so no new array; **a mask outside the Node-clock, which Postulate 5 refuses in an act: open, 13.2 item A1**); with dials below the full rate the now slot's time sits in the out and not in the state (the load never lays such dials; a world that does would need tn from the log too).
   - **So one function, tick, steps both clocks** (measured on 7b's act), and the backward clock runs it on the mirrored state, with the forward log's fire where counts are staggered. The mirrored state being T of the forward one is the Boss's ground for the dial alone (10.7); David's count of a whole second state is what the sweeps hold before the history agrees (reading 24).
   - **A flag for the count's scale against the ruler's range** (the Boss; David's open item): light and matter at amplitude 600 drive the time gear past the ruler's last count (3,792) near tick 53 and to 191,513 by tick 120, and matter alone at 900 passes it between ticks 40 and 50; T stays exact through it, but the swing does not turn there. The source's scale against the spring is owed before stage 11's bang runs.
   Also owed on the design: the count (reading 24), the shared hand from the act at the birth, form B's three passes on a small board, and a loop of two steps.
3. **Two nodes in nature** (the Boss's reading, with his dates): physics already computes real-time quantum dynamics on a forward and a backward copy of every field (Schwinger, 1961; Keldysh, 1964, the closed time path) and with two states (Aharonov, Bergmann and Lebowitz, 1964; the same precedent as 10.5, item 3). No click reads the backward half, only the correlations it makes at the split click, so "one Node-clock with two dials" and "two nodes at one place" read the same in the record. The wording is the owner's: the law keeps decision 28's wording until he says otherwise (the Boss's request). Animate's item 5 of 10.5, a dial forward from the bang and a dial back from the clicks to come, is the same thought in clocks.
4. **Read against the law** (reading 21): nothing crossed.
5. **The tag's count** (David, 10:37, comment 6494401661, under the Boss's refinement): 6 per twin train, the frame in transit (4 whole numbers for a quaternion) and sigma_b at its two outcomes, and 2 per channel train, the merge's total and the branch drawn, so at most 6 T, fixed for the run. His 5 G + 2 T + 3 of 10:28, a whole second state, is withdrawn: it was the axis of two that the refinement replaces (reading 24).

**Decision 31** (the owner's word to the Boss, relayed at 10:10, comment 6493855395; in Hebrew; in translation): "Better to see it as one board with doubled Node-clocks, which simulates a double board, no? Let the clicks be derived exactly from the full picture." The Boss's entry (tensor24-review e024b3385, 9bc7264eb): one board, one geometry, one set of Meshes and one great clock, every Node-clock doubled into a forward half and a backward half, every read within the six neighbours, each half reading the same half of its neighbours: decision 28's two boards on one Node-clock as the build holds them. The keeper brought the wording to it: the law's clause **One board with doubled Node-clocks** (ALGEBRA.md line 35, no line moved), 0.2 item 2 and 10.1, "forward board" and "backward board" staying as the names of the forward halves and the backward halves.

**The two clicks derived on the doubled Node-clock** (the Boss's; stated on the doubled Node-clock and shown beside the engine, owed on the act's own arrays at stage 9):
- **The whole click (how much):** the forward half's drive carry at the train's winding writes the record; the backward half confirms it as a check on the forward record and writes nothing (Animate's reading 12).
- **The split click (which):** at the Node-clock where the parts were one the backward half brings the basis of the clicks to come (the detectors' frames, or the absorbers' takes) with its own weights sigma_b; the forward half's hand read in that basis gives sigma_f, each the Gaussian norm of a whole-number overlap; the draw is law 9's over sigma_f sigma_b, ordered by cause (B).
- **One rule for both cases:** separate parts give decision 29's product of sizes, Born where the future is silent; a pair gives the squared overlaps of the shared hand with the two frames (tensor24-review 70ea1991b: S = -82/29, nothing typed but the frames).

**Decisions 33 and 34** (the owner's words to the Boss, relayed at 10:37 and 10:38; comments 6494395924 and 6494421600; tensor24-review bc98d3e75 and c938961e4; in Hebrew; in translation: "No need for a flag..." and "The picture closes without a flag. It is not really a doubled Node-clock, is it? Because there is one hand..?"): the Boss read the first as the universe file's switch and told the owner so, and the second confirms it. **No switch:** no file switches the backward run, and every Node-clock of every world runs both ways; decision 29's product draw and take by way (b) stay. **One hand:** each Node-clock has one hand, its gears' state; the backward run is that same hand run back, the same tick on the mirrored state (the T check, item 2), and what it carries is a tag, not a hand: the basis and the weights of the clicks to come, carried back one Mesh a step to the Node-clock where the parts were one. The split click is where the forward reading of the one hand meets the tag that came back; the whole click and the record are the forward run's alone. "Two boards" and "a doubled Node-clock" name the run's two directions, and the new array is the tag alone. The law's wording is brought to it (ALGEBRA.md line 35, **One board, one Node-clock, one hand run both ways**; line 504 without its exception; lines 92 and 1173 mended). **Asked of David by the Boss:** any case in his derivations where the backward run must carry more than a tag, a hand of its own; if none, k_b is the tag's count.

**Status:** the design is the Boss's and not yet built; the owner's words of 10:3x (decisions 33 and 34) settled the picture, no switch and one hand run both ways, and are entered in the law (line 35); the T check is in (item 2); the tag's count is David's, at most 6 T (item 5; reading 24 settled).

### How the one hand run both ways fits into the engine as built, and whether its two runs turn together (10.7)

The owner's word to the Boss of 10:1x (relayed at 10:12, comment 6493891638; in Hebrew; in translation): "In the full picture, let there be how this fits into the current engine. So in a Node there are two clocks... can they switch direction together, or each on its own?" ## 11. Ever more complex clocks, machines, and machines that make machines

**The ratchet** (clocks):
1. The clicks go only forward.
2. The room is fixed (the cap), so what grows is the arrangement. A trillion composite clocks at once need a board of side 7,937, with one matter train.
3. Gravity gathers.
4. Each bang sifts: the weakly bound break into light, and the strongly bound survive.
5. The remainders and the records pass to the next cycle.

**What is built,** in the three registers:
- **A composite clock** (derived): hands that share a dial. A bound pair runs as one clock, its beat the sum of its parts' (fusion: a split click, then a whole click). In tensors, its parts are slots of the (G, N) arrays at Node-clocks that a binding couples.
- **A machine** (a definition): a composite clock whose clicks lay hands that change its own later clicks, so it acts by what it has recorded. In acts, its whole clicks' lays (the c_j) write slots that enter its own later sources J.
- **A machine that makes machines** (a definition): a machine whose lays assemble other composites that are machines in turn. Examples are chains of givers and takers: a Sun that gives light by fusion's clicks, an Earth that takes it, and a man, a detector that records and acts by what he recorded.

**What the picture gives** (the owner's question of 06:43 UTC):
- **The conditions are derived:**
  - variation: the split click's draws read the remainders, so the choices differ;
  - memory: the quotients written go only forward, and composites persist;
  - selection: the cap's fixed room, and each bang's sifting;
  - a flow from givers to takers: light from bound givers, with the redshift's dark sky as the sink.
- **Not derived:** that a composite that copies itself arises from the act's bindings, or how often. That is a count for stages 9 and 11: per swing, the composites whose lays bind into composites like themselves. A copy is a composite of train X whose own whole clicks lay hands that bind into a new composite of the same train X, the same parts on its card (G2); per bang it is row 46's third reading (David, 6490839743, item 4).
- **The bang breaks every machine** (derived on paper).
  - Light sent in the machines' era comes back to the bottom blue-shifted by at least e^U >= 1,100, so visible light returns as X-rays, and chemically bound machines break.
  - What passes is their records, as whole numbers in the state (Postulate 4).
  - So "ever more complex" means a richer seed each cycle, not machines that survive.
- **The goal is written in no act;** it follows from the ratchet. Postulate 7 states it as the general idea (ALGEBRA.md line 35, the owner's word of 09:2x), and the principle's item (9) says how it is reached, as Sofer entered it on the owner's word of 06:3x UTC: the great clock starts from small clocks all alike, and builds clocks of more kinds and more parts by its clicks and its carries alone, nothing declared.

## 12. How it converges: the balance of light and matter

With the whole click blind to the notch (5.1), only light's history changes the whole clicks over the swing (derived on paper):
- **In the expansion,** light reddens (its local frequency falls as N against bound clocks) and thins. Whole clicks fade, and light's share of the push falls by itself, as nature's radiation thins faster than matter.
- **In the contraction,** the reverse. At the bottom, bonds break into light, and the push strengthens.

So one count, the balance of light and matter over the swing, decides three things:
1. how strong each bang is;
2. whether each cycle is larger than the last, as in the head's record;
3. the late acceleration's one door: a source that grows during the expansion, which is matter turned into light, as in Suns.
   - In nature, starlight holds about one part in a hundred thousand of matter's energy, so that door looks too narrow.
   - The late acceleration stays the named difference (**Dark energy, an accelerated parting: the algebra does not reach it and says the opposite**).

**Status:** the sign is derived on paper; the size is counted at stage 9, per swing. **And as the clicks see 7b's swing the expansion accelerates (q near -2); decision 36 (12:12) puts the time gear under the shared ruler, derived in toys, open by name and owed on the act, no contradiction booked (section 12.3).**

## 13. Tests

**Against nature:**
- **Gravity's reach is above about 4.4 billion light-years,** if the bang nature shows is the swing's bottom: 13.8 billion years have passed, the expansion goes on, and the swing's period is 2 pi times light's crossing of the reach.
- **The late acceleration,** the named difference: the swing accelerates early and slows before the top; under David's decision of 11:15 the course decelerates toward the top, and only a push dimming slower than e^-4U accelerates it (line 432's door), which the clicks' light does not give (his part C, 6495192824).
- **One seed, looking back:** consistent with the swing, and no proof of it.
- **Bell with no window:** the correlation at every distance and timing, the settings free of the pair (section 10; the retired reading's window prediction is withdrawn with the closure).
- **Gravity's tensor half** (David's flag of 10:28; the law's finding "one polarisation of the metric's waves", ALGEBRA.md line 160): the time gear is one scalar load per Node-clock, so its waves are scalar, where nature's gravitational waves carry two tensor polarizations (LIGO and Virgo since 2017; GW170814 favours tensor over scalar) and the binary pulsars' decay follows the tensor quadrupole formula; the light half, bending and delay with light at N^2, matches; the Mesh's tension (line 691) is not built on 7b and no train of the kinds E + T2 is sourced by the stress in the picture. A test against nature, owed by name, and for the fresh hostile reader. David's refinement (6494805470): the law's spring clause names the regulator's spatial block (A1 + E + T2) as the metric's spin-2 part, kept massless, so the law has a place for the tensor waves; src/tensor24 builds the time gear alone, so the flag stands for the build and its carrier is the law's own block.
- **The hierarchy** (the Boss's large-number check of 11:30, 6495446181; on his word, his scripts not in the repository): at 7b's gearing gravity has no free weight, so Gamma is the hierarchy, alpha_g about 1.17 w^2 / Gamma, and with the swing alpha_g N_t^2 about 0.585 w^4; nature has 10^30 to 10^43 and the act allows at most 57. **If the swing is the cosmic cycle, open by name under decision 36 (the large-number relation, never booked as a contradiction),** conditional on that reading; owed: David's read, and the ruler's ceiling under (II) (12.3 items 21 and 27), which may change the gearing; the scripts are committed (derive/boss/large, tensor24-review 8a476db17).
- **No horizon** (David's list of 11:17, 6495192824; derived on his page, not run): under Postulate 6 N = e^-U is never 0, so a compact mass has a photon sphere at r = 2M, isotropic, and the shadow's impact parameter is 2 e M = 5.44 M against Kerr-Schwarzschild's 5.20 M, 4.7 percent larger; EHT's shadows hold about 10 percent today and LIGO's ringdowns test the horizon. A test to read, owed by name, and for the fresh hostile physicist.

**In the engine** (stages 7b, 9 and 11):

### The numbers of nature and what a file may hold: nothing declared (13.1)

The owner's word to the Boss, relayed at 10:27 (comment 6494188814; in Hebrew; in translation): "Check whether, in the current state, it allows deriving mass ratios in nature, or other numbers." The Boss's first check, the documents (comment 6494627873, 10:48; the keeper checked the four quotes against the law's lines 1696, 1698, 1760 and 340): **no mass ratio and no coupling is derived in the current state, by the law's own words.** The Boss's second check, the act's own numbers on 7b (comment 6494791754, 11:0x; a subagent of his session ran it and he ran it again, all seven outputs equal byte for byte; the scripts are in his scratchpad, numbers_act/): **no mass ratio comes from the act; every rest frequency is the pinion's.**

**The owner's decision 35** (relayed at 10:54, comment 6494759877; in Hebrew; in translation): "You wrote 'declared', but declaring is forbidden: the muon, the tau, the quarks, W, Z and the Higgs, not derived, some declared in the file and some open. They are to appear by themselves in the clicks." The Boss's reading, entered as the rule: no particle, mass or coupling is declared; a universe file that declares one, a train's pinion standing for a particle's mass, breaches nothing declared, and that is no status. **The status words change, as the Boss asks:** "declared by the file" leaves the ledger; a number is derived (a), fitted (c, a breach by name: a number chosen to make a match), or **not yet seen in the clicks** (d, open or a contradiction by name).

**By number, in the new words:**
- m_p / m_e: not yet seen in the clicks; the head's worlds fit it (sine ratios 1,838.68 and 1,836.15, "SHOWN as a fit"; the chart's den gives 163), a fit being a breach by name. m_mu / m_e, m_tau / m_mu, the quarks, W, Z and the Higgs: not yet seen in the clicks. alpha, the generations ("absent by name", line 340), G m_e^2 / (hbar c) ("the hierarchy is the file's one number") and the CMB's uniformity: not yet seen in the clicks. Omega_Lambda and q0: a contradiction by name ("+0.15 against nature's -0.55"; 12.3).
- **Derived, with nothing typed:** light at 1/sqrt 6; the 24 and the five kinds; rho + 3p; Bell's -82/29, on paper and beside the engine; the form of Planck's key, though not its value.

**What the act fixes by itself, and what it does not** (the Boss's second check): the 24 (5 classes, of sizes 1, 3, 6, 6 and 8); the kinds (dimensions 1, 1, 2, 3 and 3); the 43 tables, each of multiplicity one; the cone, s grip / W = 1/6 for any den and Gamma, exactly the leapfrog's stability limit; the source rho + 3p (over the energy 2.001 for light and 1.000 for matter); the time gear's rest frequency sqrt(2 / Gamma) (0.0447 a tick at Gamma 1000). **Masses:** cos omega = pinion / den exactly; all 24 file rows at one pinion (A1 and A2, T1 and T2, hands, a mixed train) give one omega to 5e-15, since the layout holds one W, one s and one D for every row, so in 7b the kinds are mass-blind; nature's lepton and proton pinions put in come back as put in, with nothing added. **Couplings:** about a still background the coupled modes follow (2 / W) (D - strength x background x table) to 1e-14; their ratios move with the strengths and the background the setting chooses; the tables fix only how a multiplet splits in a background (2 : -1 : -1 along (1, 1, 1)), a ratio of shifts and not of masses. **Against nature:** 3,322 of the act's numbers against five targets gave 17 hits within 1 percent, against 17.1 expected by chance (43^2 = 1,849 against 1,836: +0.7 percent): coincidences by count, none of those numbers entering a frequency. **So:** a particle's mass can appear by itself only as the beat of a composite the clicks build (stage 9's binding, the ladder from one seed); a train cannot give it; David's ladder spectrum is the step (the Boss). A side finding for the build, owed when it resumes: the time gear's drive tooth rd is laid at 0 and the drive's carry floors, so the first negative source kicks the time gear down a whole kick; laying rd at its home would make the response symmetric, with a test that fails on the floor's bias.

**David's answer** (6494401661, item 6): he holds no derivation of a mass ratio of nature from the act; his mass page derives what a mass is, one Gaussian integer g = pinion + i s per train, and not its value; a no-go as built, every rest turn a train's declared pair or a sum over bound parts. **The one door:** binding's share, if the 43 tables' whole-number factors fix a bound composite's beat below the sum of its parts with no file number, a count for stage 9. The numbers the act does fix with no file: light's 1/sqrt 6, the 24's five kinds and 43 tables, the closed composites' counts, Bell's -82/29 and Tsirelson's 478/169; none is a mass ratio.

**The three targets** (the owner's word to the Boss, relayed at 10:52, comment 6494709668; in Hebrew; in translation): "The three generations, Planck's constant and a large number: let them try to find them from the tensors, from the clocks, from how we must solve problems, and from the dual board... that the past converges to the future... and to remember that it is in the clicks." **Open items, each "not yet seen in the clicks":** (1) the three generations; (2) Planck's key, its form derived (one key for every train) and its value not; (3) a large pure number, the law having one scale, Gamma, and numbers of order one (line 1760). The Boss's four sources for each: the tensors (the 24, the five kinds, the 43 tables), the clocks (the carry, the gears, the time gear and the swing), the method (the principle of the clock and the gear: each term from the one act, nothing declared, the books first) and the dual run (the one hand run forward and back, the history agreeing with itself at both ends); his lead, against the law's line 1698 ("a fixed point of the whole that selects Gamma or a ratio of the weights by itself, the law has none"): the history that agrees with itself is a fixed point of the whole history, and the owner's "the past converges to the future" asks whether it selects Gamma, the key or a ratio. **The rule:** no fitted number (a match to nature counts only if no number was chosen to make it) and the tries counted, so that a coincidence is named as one. David writes a page per target, Animate the record's reading for each, the Boss three probes beside the engine (running); the status words enter here as they come. **The fixed point** (the owner's word to the Boss, relayed at 11:0x, comment 6494837215: "Search in the fixed point of future and past, and everything that needs to be seen in the clocks, in the tensors and in the great clock that produces coherent clicks." The Boss runs a probe on these four beside the three running (each of which also searches the fixed point); David derives (ii) and (iv) in clocks, asking whether the return map has a fixed point and whether the agreement of the two bangs makes the bang uniform; Animate reads the spread of the bang's ticks across Node-clocks and the height of each swing's top. Each stays "not yet seen in the clicks" until a probe posts. **The record's readings for the fixed point** (Animate, comment 6495846565, item 2): the spread of the bang's ticks across Node-clocks: per Node-clock the round of its own bottom, from the time gear's line per Node-clock at "step" (G1 per site), per bang the spread of those rounds (the latest less the earliest) against the swing's period, 0 for a uniform bang and a sixth for the Boss's 8^3 board's tops, and with G7 every click reads its own Node-clock's load, so the clicks read the spread a second time; the height of each swing's top: per swing the board's load at its top (the largest summed load between two bottoms, G1) and per Node-clock its own top, the return map being the pairs (top n, top n + 1) and a fixed point two equal tops, in whole numbers; NOT READ where a swing is not whole in the record.)

**The large-number check** (the Boss, comment 6495446181, item 3, 11:30; a subagent of his session ran it and he ran ruler.py and closure.py again, both outputs equal; committed at tensor24-review 8a476db17 (12:24) as derive/boss/large, and run again by the keeper with python -I, every output equal byte for byte, closure.py in 82 seconds): **no large pure number of the act's own:** every large number is a power of Gamma, with one logarithm from the ruler, or of den or a pinion; the ruler's length has an exact closed form, about (Gamma / 2) ln(2 Gamma), equal to the walk at 415 values of Gamma. **A quantum's depth:** about 0.217 of a notch per Node-clock at w = 0.2, the same at every Gamma, which answers the open item of section 4. **The fixed point:** the agreement at both ends of the gears is an identity of the act, since the run back is exact on every history, the runaway included, so it excludes nothing and fixes nothing (candidates (i) and (iii)); a strict closure, a later bang equal to the past one, picks seeds and not Gamma (candidate (ii): the time gear returns after 1 to 117 swings, erratically, and with matter the state never repeats in 30,000 ticks); its selecting power, if any, is at the split clicks (the tag and the draw) and not in the gears. The owner's word to the Boss at 13:5x (the head list): the whole team hunts for holes in the final plan until 14:45 UTC, in two directions (back from nature, and forward from the seed), and the owner approves the final picture at 15:00 UTC. **The keeper's area** (the Boss's list, 6498614087, item 3): every line of 0.2 against the law's lines and decisions 25 to 39, naming contradictions between decisions; anything still declared or apart in the code, the files or the plan; the open list complete, with nothing called closed that is not; line 1841 under decision 39 (done). **How it was done:** two read-only hostile readers reported at 14:2x (decisions 25 to 39 against each other and the law, fourteen findings; the code, the files, the tests and the probes for what is declared or apart, fifteen findings); the keeper read the quotes of each finding entered here against the source, and enters nothing the source does not carry; the keeper's own pass read 0.2's fifteen lines against the law (three statements made exact in 0.2 items 4 and 15). 1. **The whole click** as 5.1 writes it, with the channel's source carrying the ruler's factor, F(c) = K Gamma^2 div Y(c)^2, at teeth K T_c fixed for the run, and every coupling row's drive with its own factor by the same paragraph of the law.

3. **The take on light's gears,** way (b), the owner's by decision 29, scoped to light that holds many quanta at the Node-clock.
4. **The per-swing readings** of section 13, at stages 9 and 11.
5. **The architect's holes for the picture** (the Boss's 6491347414, by stage), each read against the law by the keeper (08:2x UTC):
   - **Stage 9, proper time in the record.** A click must carry its Node-clock's count. The law agrees: each Node-clock steps on its own clock (**The Node-clock steps on its own clock**) and the click is logged at its Node-clock; the record then reads the time dilation that the decoder cannot read from the beat dial. No law crossed.
   - **Stage 9, the sign of q.** The law's click is the carry's quotient at least 1 (the principle's item (4): q = 0 no click, q at least 1 the click, no comparison); a quotient below 0 is no click in the law, so the record writes q at least 1 and the signed quotient is the act's, the click then the same take with the giver and the receiver exchanged (the Boss's ruling of A3 and A4). To write a click for a negative quotient would need the owner's word.
   - **Stage 9, light's winding.** A channel whose teeth would be 0 is a zero divisor in the drive's carry; the law's carry has d at least 1 (n + r = q d + r' with 0 <= r' < d), so the load refuses it (the guard); M2 closes by refusal. No law crossed.
   - **Stage 9, the take's reach.** The take acts on the Node-clock's own gears alone, as the law says (line 92: "the quantum taken, one whole unit of the light at the Node-clock and its six Spokes and nothing beyond"); a take on a gradient or force coupling reads two Meshes away, which the load refuses. The law and the build agree.
   - **Stage 9, the draw's digits.** The law's draw reads the Node-clock's own remainders and one Mesh's digit (**The click's read of the great clock**); the build's V sums the Node-clock's and its six neighbours' digits, still within six neighbours; the form differs from the law's wording (reading 1), and a neighbour's r or rd needs a slot of its own.
   - **Stage 9, two new state arrays:** the windings and the take's kept per train and Node-clock, so 5 G + 2 T + 3 (the description's 5 G + T + 3 counts the windings alone), and the backward run's tag on top, at most 6 T (decisions 28, 33 and 34); line 98 fixes the count for the run and names no number. No law crossed.
   - **Stage 10b, the ledger.** With the click inside the one act, "after the step" and "after the clicks" are one state, so row 15's second reading could never fail; a check that cannot fail is no check, and the owner's rule is the books first (the principle's item (6)), so the Out must expose the click's writes or the ledger must read before and after the round against the books' work.
   - **Stage 11, the bang rows** (1, 14, 34, 46, 61) count the time gear's count swing and no Turnover line, which no act writes since decision 27: the law agrees (**Time in the engine is a circle**, every bang shows more).
   - **Animate, the Turnover record:** it stays for the converter alone (her 6491358590), the head's records being made under its act at the top.

The owner's words to the Boss, relayed at 13:0x (comment 6497161018; in Hebrew; in translation): "All the exclusions and fakes will be thrown out, so that the base is right. - **G2, the great clock as families:** the lapse and the ruler (A1, A1), each an ordinary family with the one law; their sources as rows of the one plan (rho + 3p, rho - p), with the free-count tie; read: the wells add (gamma = 1, derived), q per era (E7), the waves on light's cone, the exact run back, the 24.
